"Because people sign before asking where Mirror Spring is."

"I asked."

"That's why good."

I left.

Perrin was waiting by market fountain.

"You went office."

"I enjoy ruining stories with paperwork."

"Grandfather is at Morrow tomorrow morning."

"I have no money for cart."

"He'll pay cart if he wants you."

"That sounds suspiciously like free fare."

"It's employment travel."

Damn technically legitimate economics.

"I haven't accepted."

"I know."

He stopped pushing.

Good.

"Can he come here?"

"He won't leave rows."

Human problem again.

"Then I either spend work time going to him or don't."

"Yes."

"Any work here tomorrow early?"

"Glass yard always has something."

Not guarantee.

I went work board.

**GLASS RETURN CRATE PAD / 2C / FIRST RING / SEATED OK**

There.

Not exciting.

But enough to restore floor margin before deciding.

I marked it mentally.

No claim yet.

At Glass Yard floor, kiln glow painted ceiling orange through high windows.

I opened notebook.

Wrote:

**Potential actual job: Morrow Orchard, Teren Sol. Old story route White Reed -> Sleeping Stone -> Three Sisters -> Mirror Spring. 8c route notes; 12c with sealed supplemental spring sample. District confirms low east channels + sample accepted as supplemental only. Old path unmaintained; current accessibility unknown; landslide near Three Sisters. Lower rows may lose fruit in 2-3 days.**

Then:

**This is somebody else's problem.**

I stared.

Added:

**Which is usually how adventure starts.**

Annoying.

No quest accepted.

Not yet.

But for first time since leaving Carrow, tomorrow had something more specific than next town.

A person.

A place.

A path.

A reason to reach end and come back.

That felt different.

Wayfinder.

Not because I knew way.

Because story did.

Maybe.

# CHAPTER 459

## THE CONTRACTOR

Adventure started with crate padding.

Naturally.

Lakeward Glass Yard first ring smelled like wet wood, ash, and somebody else's expensive mistake.

I had ten copper.

Exactly ten.

Floor had stopped being policy and become current account balance.

Twenty-three Vale remained separate.

Leg money remained leg money.

Mirror Spring remained a sentence in notebook.

Before I let sentence become several days of bad terrain, I wanted two things.

Money.

Then Teren Sol's actual mouth.

Not Perrin's summary of it.

Glass Return Crate Pad paid two copper for one ring.

I took it.

Supervisor **Hala Renn** pointed at bench.

"Returned crates only. Pull old reed pad. Check frame for obvious split. New pad goes corners and base. If frame split, red chalk and stop. You are not a cratewright."

"I am collecting professions I am not."

"Good. Collect quietly."

Trial leg had rested.

Dry bench floor.

But I was leaving town after if job existed.

Unknown route.

Save skin.

Crutches.

I sat.

Crate padding was satisfying in way repetitive work sometimes became when endpoint obvious.

Old reed out.

Frame look.

No split.

New reed in.

Tie.

Stack.

Second crate had corner separation.

Red chalk.

Stop.

Third smelled like vinegar.

"Problem?" I asked.

Hala sniffed.

"Cleaning return. Fine."

Fourth had glass dust under base seam.

Question.

She swept sample onto black card.

"Old break residue. Crate cleared yesterday. Pad replacement only."

Fine.

No mystery.

No quest in crate.

Good.

At end of ring she counted two copper.

Ten to twelve.

I liked twelve better.

Still thin.

But floor had breathing room.

I found Perrin at east cart stand carrying graft knives in wrapped roll and arguing with man about axle space.

Not angrily.

Persistently.

"It's one roll."

"One roll becomes twelve rolls when everybody says one roll."

"Mine actually is one."

"That's what makes it dangerous."

Perrin saw me.

"You came."

"I came to ask your grandfather whether you invented his money."

"I rounded."

"Up?"

"No."

Interesting.

Cart master found knife roll space under bench for one bit.

Perrin paid.

"Morrow cart?" I asked.

"Second ring."

"Fare?"

"Two copper. Grandfather covers if you're coming about spring."

"He said that?"

"He said bring anyone willing to listen before talking."

"That's not same sentence."

Perrin exhaled.

"No. It isn't."

Good.

"Then I talk first."

"Fine."

I paid nothing.

We waited for cart.

Perrin did not fill silence well.

He tried anyway.

"The story isn't exactly how my grandfather tells it."

"Already encouraging."

"He says Iria didn't clear the spring."

There.

"What does he say?"

"She found where water went."

"Different."

"Yes."

"Where did it go?"

"Depends which time he tells it."

Better.

"And you want me to work for this man."

"I want somebody to go before lower pears drop."

That was honest.

"Why you?"

"Because my mother won't leave rows. Grandfather's knee won't take Three Sisters. My sisters are eleven and eight. Graft house already gave me yesterday afternoon because ferry was late. If I disappear three days, I lose placement."

"Could lose fruit instead."

"Yes."

No clean answer.

Cart arrived.

Morrow Orchard cart was flat farm wagon with canvas half roof and bins of empty produce trays going east.

Driver **Nemi Cross** checked Perrin's pass.

Perrin pointed at me.

"Possible spring hire."

Nemi said, "Possible doesn't ride account."

Correct.

Perrin opened mouth.

I said, "I'll pay two. If Teren hires me and travel is covered, he reimburses. If not, my trip."

Ten-floor problem.

Twelve to ten.

Exactly floor again.

But this was deliberate information cost, not casual spending.

Was that rational?

Maybe.

Could have demanded Teren come Lakeward.

But orchard clock mattered.

And I wanted to see channels.

Paid two.

Twelve to ten.

Adventure apparently hated liquidity.

Cart east.

Lakeward glass roofs receded.

Road climbed gently through orchards.

Rows first sparse.

Then continuous.

Pear-like trees? Avoid Earth exact maybe fruit types can be pears established by Perrin line. Fine.

Low stone channels ran beside road.

Some wet.

Some barely.

Workers lifted small wooden gates between terraces.

Not one irrigation system.

Many linked ones.

At first mile channel had steady finger-deep flow.

Second less.

Near Morrow, water moved thin over dark stone.

Not dry.

Low.

I crouched at public road crossing.

Did not touch.

Perrin said, "Yesterday higher."

"Measured?"

"Not by me."

Good correction.

"Who?"

"Gate keeper chalk board."

Morrow Orchard wasn't one farm.

Cluster of family holdings around packing shed, graft house, drying yard, and shared lower channel.

Morrow apparently district name older than current families.

Teren Sol sat under shade roof repairing basket handles.

Late sixties maybe.

Broad shoulders reduced by age rather than erased.

Left knee wrapped.

He looked at my crutches.

Then at Perrin.

"That's who you found?"

Perrin stiffened.

I liked Teren less immediately.

Useful.

"He found me," I said.

Teren looked at me again.

"Can you walk Three Sisters?"

"Don't know."

"Then why are you here?"

"To find out what you're actually hiring for before I decide whether your bad knee and my missing foot should negotiate terrain by proxy."

Perrin looked at ground.

Teren barked laugh.

Not warm.

But real.

"Sit."

I sat.

He did not know Peg-Leg Greg.

Excellent.

"Perrin says eight for route notes, twelve with sealed spring sample."

"Eight if you reach source and return useful notes. Twelve with district bottle filled and sealed correctly."

"Travel?"

"Lakeward to Morrow reimbursed if hired."

Good.

"Supplies?"

"Two days food. One blanket roll. Bottle deposit. Route copy."

"Two days?"

"Old path is day up, day back."

"When your knee worked?"

Pause.

"Yes."

"How long ago?"

"Nine years."

There.

Perrin looked away.

"Landslide?"

"Six years."

"So two days is story estimate, not current route estimate."

Teren's jaw moved.

"Three if slow."

"Unknown if blocked."

"Yes."

"Unknown if passable on crutches."

"Obviously."

"Not obvious enough if you're pricing two days food."

Perrin coughed into hand.

Teren stared.

Then said, "Three days food."

Good.

Not victory.

Basic planning.

"Turnaround condition?"

"What?"

"When do I stop trying and come back?"

"If path gone."

"Define gone."

"You enjoy this?"

"More than falling."

He rubbed wrapped knee.

"If Three Sisters lower bench is cut through, don't climb north face. Old upper track crosses loose shelf."

Specific.

"Any water crossing?"

"White Reed ford. Usually ankle. Could be knee after rain."

"Current?"

"Don't know."

"Weather?"

Perrin answered. "Dry two days. Ridge fog mornings. No rain posted."

Teren nodded.

"Sleeping Stone shelter?"

"Overhang. Dry unless east wind."

"People on route?"

"White Reed keeper sometimes. Three Sisters nobody permanent. Mirror Spring old catch house abandoned."

Now adventure.

"Why not send district team?"

"They are coming."

"Then why me?"

Teren leaned forward.

"Because three days is three days. Because lower fruit doesn't care whose seal is on paper. Because if source is running strong, I want to know before Halden families close another gate and call it conservation. Because if source is weak, I want Perrin's mother to stop accusing people who may not be stealing anything."

There.

Human problem.

"And because you want old path proven."

His eyes narrowed.

Perrin had warned me.

"I want people to remember there was a path before district road decided water begins where measuring box is."

"That's different job."

"Same mountain."

"Not same job."

Teren got angry quietly.

"You think because office draws new line the old one stops carrying water?"

"No. I think if you pay me to inspect source, I need to know whether you're paying for evidence or vindication."

Silence.

Perrin finally said, "Grandfather."

Teren looked at him.

Perrin kept going.

Messier now.

"This is what I meant. You keep saying walk it, walk it, walk it like if somebody puts feet on every old stone then everyone has to admit you were right about closing the lower marker council, and that's not what we need right now. We need to know if water is there. We need to know if something happened above Three Sisters. Mom doesn't care if the old path gets a plaque. She cares if Row Four drops fruit."

Teren's face changed.

Not defeated.

Hurt.

Good.

Somebody else's story.

Teren said, "Your mother learned that channel on my back."

"I know."

"You learned White Reed because I took you."

"I know."

"Then don't talk like memory is decoration."

"I'm not. I'm saying pears are dying while you argue about memory."

No sarcasm academy.

No clean winner.

I waited.

Eventually Teren looked at me.

"Evidence," he said.

"Route notes too?"

"Yes."

"Separate sections."

He frowned.

"What?"

"One page: route condition. One page: water observations. No mixing `old marker missing` with `upper family stole water`."

"Fine."

"Sample supplemental only."

"Fine."

"I do not accuse Halden families for you."

"Fine."

"I do not prove story."

Longer pause.

"Fine."

Perrin exhaled.

I wasn't done.

"Pay. Twelve with valid sealed sample. Eight if source reached and route notes returned but sample invalid or impossible. Four if I reach Three Sisters and route beyond is unsafe under agreed turnaround, with notes showing where and why."

Teren said, "Four for failing?"

"Four for useful route information that prevents next person from discovering same failure."

He hated it.

Thought.

"Three."

"Four."

"Three and your cart fare back."

"Four and cart fare already paid reimbursed now if hired."

Perrin whispered, "He's going to die bargaining before trail."

Teren ignored him.

"Four. Fare reimbursed."

"Good."

Contract.

Not paper yet.

We went packing shed office.

Morrow clerk wrote terms.

**MIRROR SPRING SUPPLEMENTAL WALK**

Employer: Morrow lower-channel cooperative, signed Teren Sol under emergency field-observation allowance? Need not invent legal detail too much. Better private cooperative job with district sample form separate.

Terms:

- route: Morrow -> White Reed -> Sleeping Stone -> Three Sisters -> Mirror Spring where safely passable;
- return route notes + water observations;
- 12c if valid district supplemental source sample returned with completed seal form;
- 8c if source reached and useful route notes returned but valid sample cannot be completed;
- 4c if Three Sisters reached and continuation judged unsafe under specified conditions, with useful route notes;
- Lakeward->Morrow 2c fare reimbursed upon hire;
- three days food + blanket roll supplied;
- district bottle deposit supplied by employer;
- no authority to alter gates, clear source, move markers, enter closed private channels, or represent district;
- Greg may turn back earlier for injury, weather, route loss, or equipment failure, but sub-Three-Sisters return has no guaranteed field pay beyond reimbursement/supplies consumed in good faith.

Good.

Teren paid my two copper reimbursement.

Ten to twelve.

Buffer restored.

He did not advance wages.

Fair.

"Bottle tomorrow," Perrin said. "District office runner comes Morrow first ring."

"Departure after?"

Teren said, "White Reed by midday if road dry."

"Road?"

"Farm track first part. Old path starts after keeper house."

"Guide?"

"Perrin can take you White Reed."

Perrin's head snapped around.

"I can?"

Teren said, "Graft house starts second ring tomorrow."

"And if cart back late?"

"Then I pay missed half."

Perrin looked suspicious.

"You hate paying missed half."

"I hate dry channels more."

There.

Short segment guide.

Not party.

Perrin could show maintained farm track to White Reed, then return for graft work if timing held.

"Does he know beyond White Reed?" I asked.

"To Sleeping Stone," Teren said.

Perrin said, "Barely. I was twelve."

"Then White Reed only."

Teren opened mouth.

I said, "You hired notes, not confidence theater."

He closed it.

Good.

Preparation.

Route sketch.

Old archive copy from Lakeward? Teren had hand copy.

Not map exactly.

Line with named markers, water branches, two old stone walls, Three Sisters contour scribble.

I compared to district archive notes copied yesterday.

Same sequence.

Different distances.

Teren's copy said White Reed to Sleeping Stone `one long climb`.

District archive said `approx. 3.4 road miles old measure`? Avoid modern miles? We have paces, road miles maybe prior. Better local distance uncertain. Use bells walking.

Teren: two bells when young.

Archive: no current time.

Fine.

I asked for physical landmarks.

White Reed: pale reed bed at channel fork.

Sleeping Stone: long dark slab over path, shaped like back under blanket.

Three Sisters: three narrow stone spires visible from lower hollow.

Mirror Spring: catch wall of black stone below white cliff seam.

Story names matched useful navigation.

Not magic.

Maybe.

Food:

flatbread.

dried fruit.

hard cheese.

smoked? Fine.

Three days modest.

Water flask.

Extra wrap.

Blanket roll.

No free expensive gear.

Teren offered old walking staff.

I looked at crutches.

"Inventory conflict."

He grunted.

Perrin laughed.

Sample bottle not yet.

Weather board at Morrow:

**RIDGE FOG MORNING / DRY DAY / EAST WIND LATE / NO RAIN WATCH**

Current only.

Three Sisters could differ.

Trial leg decision.

This mattered.

Crutches primary on unknown path.

Trial build could help on stable farm track and maybe free hands, but carrying extra weight plus unknown terrain.

Shop property.

Hark would murder me politely.

Already had been on boats.

Could leave at Morrow secured, but then if route includes stable sections I lose option.

Could carry wrapped, use only known dry stable stretches.

Weight cost.

I tested pack balance with it attached.

Manageable.

Not ideal.

Keep.

No wear today.

Skin rested.

Tomorrow maybe farm track only if dry.

Crutches on old path unless obvious stable ground.

Artifacts.

Line quietener.

Current reader.

Load tile.

Lot 67.

Did I need all?

I had been carrying them anyway.

Lot 67 could potentially compare timing if real interval question, but no reason yet.

Current reader maybe water/current direction? WS-F/9 slate current reader perhaps could be useful with flowing water, but limitations. Don't assume without state detail. Carry, characterize only if appropriate.

Load tile not obviously relevant.

Still carried.

No new purchase.

No money.

Magic.

Could test before trip.

No.

Save attention.

Protocol stayed 59 / 53 / 138.

Late afternoon Perrin took me lower channel.

Public cooperative section.

Chalk board nailed under roof.

Three readings across day.

Numbers meant local gauge marks, not flow volume.

Morning 6.2.

Midday 5.8.

Late 5.5.

Declining.

One day only.

Not trend beyond day.

Upper branch board? Different station, not visible.

"See?" Perrin said.

"I see this gauge declined today."

"You know what I mean."

"Yes. That's why I'm saying exact sentence."

He kicked dirt.

"You're irritating."

"Professionally now."

"Grandfather likes you."

"That's concerning."

"He doesn't."

"Better."

We walked rows.

Lower trees showed stress subtly.

Some leaves curled.

Fruit smaller? I couldn't know baseline.

Perrin knew.

He pointed Row Four.

"Those should be heavier by now."

"Could be water?"

"Could be heat. Could be root issue. Could be because this year's set was stupidly dense and Mom refused thinning enough because last year hail took half."

There.

Messy agriculture.

"So even source result won't explain fruit."

"No."

"Good."

He looked offended.

"Not good good. Evidence good."

"You talk like clerk."

"I've been paid as several."

At row end, older woman stood by gate.

Perrin's mother.

**Sera Sol**.

She had pruning knife and expression of person already informed about me and unconvinced.

"You're the walker."

"Apparently."

She looked at missing lower leg, crutches, pack.

Did not hide assessment.

"Teren sent a one-legged stranger up an abandoned path because he's too stubborn to wait for district."

"I negotiated."

"That doesn't improve sentence."

Fair.

Perrin said, "Mom."

"What? It's accurate."

I liked her more than Teren.

"Do you want me not to go?" I asked.

She answered too quickly.

"I want water."

Then slower:

"I want nobody hurt because my father thinks old stories are maps."

"Archive says places are real."

"Places being real doesn't make path safe."

"Agreed."

"If Three Sisters is bad, turn around."

"Contract says that."

"Contract is my father's handwriting with a clerk making it respectable. I'm telling you as person whose son will blame himself if you break your neck after he brought you here."

Perrin: "I wouldn't."

She looked at him.

"You absolutely would."

He shut up.

There.

Quest had people now.

Not just objective.

"I'll turn if route is beyond me," I said.

Sera nodded.

"And if spring is running strong?"

"I record that."

"Even if it means upper gates?"

"It means source appears strong at observation point. Upper gates would be separate claim."

She closed eyes briefly.

"Gods, my father hired a sentence."

"Twelve copper if sentence comes back with bottle."

She almost smiled.

Almost.

Then said, "Come eat. If you're walking tomorrow, I'm not letting Teren's travel ration be your dinner."

Food hospitality.

Not equipment windfall.

I accepted.

Family table.

Perrin's sisters **Lio** and **Mina** asked whether crutches made uphill harder.

"Yes."

"How much?"

"Depends hill."

"That's not number."

"Correct."

Mina asked if I was actually called Peg-Leg Greg.

Perrin had told them.

"No."

"But people call you that."

"Unfortunately."

"Where's peg?"

"Excellent question."

Sera looked at Perrin.

Perrin became fascinated by bread.

Teren arrived late.

He and Sera barely spoke about water.

That told me they had spoken too much already.

After dinner Teren brought small black pebble.

Not magical-looking.

Smooth.

"From Mirror Spring?" I asked.

"My father said."

"You know?"

"No."

Good.

He put it on table.

"I used to carry it when I walked route."

"Want me to take it?"

He hesitated.

"No."

Better.

"Why show me?"

"So you understand I'm not asking because of a children's story."

"You're asking because story is how your family remembers a route."

"And because route is how we remembered water didn't start at our gate."

There.

That was it.

Not sacred relic.

Not quest token.

Memory object.

He took pebble back.

"Bring your own rock if you need one."

I touched Tavi's striped rock in pack.

"Already overstocked."

Teren didn't understand.

Fine.

I slept in packing shed loft.

Employer lodging.

No copper.

Reasonable as hired field worker, not protagonist gift.

Cash twelve.

Ten floor.

Twenty-three Vale.

Tomorrow district runner brings bottle first ring.

Perrin takes me to White Reed.

Then turns back.

After White Reed, old path.

Sleeping Stone.

Three Sisters.

Mirror Spring if passable.

Return.

Three days food.

Twelve copper if bottle valid.

Eight if source reached without valid sample.

Four if Three Sisters proves unsafe and notes useful.

Zero guaranteed if I turn earlier except reimbursed fare and supplied food used in good faith.

That felt fair enough to be dangerous.

I wrote home note.

Longer than usual.

**Lakeward east, then Morrow Orchard. I took a short field contract. Lower orchard channels are running low. Old local story preserves a real route through White Reed, Sleeping Stone, Three Sisters Hollow, to Mirror Spring. District confirms places/source exist but old path is unmaintained and landslide affected Three Sisters. I'm being paid to return route/water observations and, if possible, a sealed supplemental spring sample. This does not make me district authority and sample will not prove cause by itself. Three days food supplied. Perrin Sol takes me only to White Reed, then returns to work. Cash 12c, Vale 23c. Ten floor intact. Trial build healthy. - Greg**

Then I added:

**This is an actual adventure now, which was more charming when theoretical.**

Postage from Morrow? Could go with morning cart. Bits.

I left note with packing clerk for Lakeward post run.

At loft, I laid gear out.

Crutches.

Pack.

Blanket.

Food.

Water.

Trial limb wrapped.

Notebook.

Artifacts.

Empty place for district bottle.

No sword suddenly appeared because chapter had become quest.

Old me found that funny.

Old me also knew most quests began with worse preparation than people admitted later.

Contractor.

That was what I was being.

Not hero.

Not chosen.

Not even sure path existed in useful form.

Somebody had a problem.

I had agreed to walk toward its source.

Tomorrow, finally, the road stopped being transportation.

It became the thing I had to survive.

# CHAPTER 460

## THE TRAILBLAZER

The bottle arrived before breakfast.

Adventure had paperwork.

Good.

The district runner set a padded wooden case on Morrow packing-shed table and made me read the seal form back to her.

Collector.

Route.

Fill point.

Observed conditions.

Witness if available.

Seal number before fill.

Seal number after fill.

Do not rinse bottle in source.

Do not touch inside neck or stopper.

Do not fill from standing pool if flowing source can be reached safely.

Do not alter source to obtain sample.

That last one mattered.

I signed receipt.

The Morrow cooperative had posted the one-copper bottle deposit already.

No money moved from me.

Cash twelve.

Vale twenty-three.

Protocol fifty-nine, fifty-three, one-thirty-eight.

Still boring.

Excellent.

The runner left.

Garrick Sol inspected the case like he expected bureaucracy to have hidden a second bottle inside out of spite.

"That's all?"

"Bottle, seal, form."

"They used to send a reader."

"They still are. Later."

"Later is useful word when your trees aren't thirsty."

Helena Sol was tying my food roll at the other end of table.

"Do not start before he leaves."

Garrick looked at her.

"I said one sentence."

"It was the first stair."

Jonah Sol came in carrying two water flasks.

That name was new.

Better.

I had spent part of last night looking at the names in my own notes and realizing the road had developed a disease.

Perrin.

Sera.

Lio.

Rusk.

Names I had already heard elsewhere, or names close enough to old ones that my notebook was starting to look like one family had colonized the continent.

So I fixed the notes before they hardened.

Jonah Sol.

Garrick Sol.

Helena Sol.

Ada and June.

Celia Brohm at Lakeward water office.

People deserved names that did not sound like I had drawn them from the same small sack of syllables.

Jonah put one flask beside my pack.

"Mine. You give it back at White Reed."

"Planning to."

"Grandfather says keeper has water."

"Grandfather says many things."

From table, Garrick said, "I can hear you."

"Good."

Helena smiled into knot.

We left after first ring.

Jonah carried his own small day bag.

I carried contract gear.

Crutches primary.

Trial limb wrapped against side of pack.

I had considered wearing it on maintained farm track.

Then looked at morning fog, damp stone, and several hours of unknown effort ahead.

No.

Save it.

The first stretch out of Morrow barely felt like quest.

Orchard rows.

Gate boxes.

Workers.

Cart ruts.

Low walls.

People called to Jonah by name.

Nobody called to me.

Good.

Water channel followed road uphill.

At Morrow it had been thin.

Above first terrace it was wider.

Not dramatically.

Enough to notice.

"Upper gate?" I asked.

Jonah pointed ahead.

"Shared split. Morrow branch takes left. Halden road takes right."

"Do we cross it?"

"Yes."

At split, wooden gates stood in stone throat.

Both open partially.

A chalk board under little roof showed morning marks.

I copied them.

Did not interpret.

Jonah watched.

"You could just say there's more water here."

"There is more water here."

"See?"

"That does not tell us why less reaches Morrow."

"I know."

"Then why are we fighting?"

"Because you make every sentence wear boots."

I liked that.

"Useful on trail."

"Terrible at dinner."

We kept climbing.

Maintained road narrowed after last packing yard.

Orchards broke into patches.

Stone walls older.

Some trees abandoned or feral.

Fruit small and hard.

Fog burned away slowly.

East wind had not started.

At a bend Jonah stopped.

"White Reed from here."

I saw pale stripe below us.

Not white exactly.

Silver-yellow reeds packed along shallow basin where two channels met.

A squat keeper house sat above ford.

Beyond it, road continued east as cart track.

Old path did not.

Old path climbed north beside reed basin and vanished into trees.

There.

Line between transport and adventure.

Of course there was a house at it.

Civilization liked putting paperwork at thresholds.

Keeper was **Mabel Crowe**.

Sixties maybe.

Short gray braid.

Mud boots.

One sleeve rolled higher than other because right forearm was wrapped in old burn scar.

She looked at Jonah.

"Garrick finally bullied somebody into walking it?"

"Contracted," I said.

She looked at me.

Then crutches.

Then pack.

"Worse."

Jonah said, "Morning, Mabel."

"You going too?"

"Only here."

"Good. Your mother has sense."

"That isn't why."

"Still good."

Different voice.

No dry-comeback academy. She spoke like conclusions had been waiting before we arrived.

I showed contract and district bottle receipt.

Mabel read slowly.

Twice.

"They put Three Sisters failure pay in writing."

"I insisted."

"Smart. Garrick would've paid you in regret."

Jonah said, "He's my grandfather."

"Then you know."

Maybe one dry line. Fine.

Mabel handed papers back.

"Ford first. Then you can decide whether old path deserves your remaining leg."

"Missing one already."

She stared.

"Yes. I counted."

Fair.

White Reed ford was not ankle deep.

Not knee either.

Mid-shin on Jonah.

Current modest but visible.

Bottom rounded stone with green film.

Bad crutch surface.

Old path crossed directly through it.

New keeper footbridge stood thirty paces downstream.

Narrow timber.

Rail one side.

"Why would I ford?" I asked.

Mabel pointed at route copy.

"Because old path did."

"Why would I care?"

Jonah laughed.

Mabel did not.

Then she smiled slightly.

"Good. Use bridge."

There.

Story route was not commandment.

The place mattered.

The exact historical foot placement did not.

I inspected bridge.

Dry enough.

Boards intact.

One-sided rail meant crutches still workable.

No trial limb.

Crossed slowly.

Fine.

On far side I went back toward ford bank without entering water.

White Reed soil.

Story said wet even when channel dry.

Current channel was not dry.

So story condition did not reproduce.

Still, something interesting.

Reed basin held water higher than incoming visible side channel suggested.

Could be groundwater.

Could be backwater geometry.

Could be seep.

Could be me not understanding basin.

Mabel crouched and pushed thin marked rod into mud near reed edge.

"You measuring?"

"Every morning."

"What?"

"Mud-water line in three holes. Not official. Mine."

"Why?"

She gave me look like question silly.

"Because reeds tell you after they're unhappy. Holes tell me before."

Good.

"Trend?"

"Lower than last week. Higher than channel makes people think."

"Meaning?"

"Meaning exactly that. Don't make me your clerk."

I wrote:

**White Reed: visible channel low relative to keeper expectation; shallow basin substrate remains wet. Keeper Mabel Crowe tracks three informal mud-water holes daily; says lower than last week, still higher than channel appearance suggests. No causal claim.**

Jonah read over shoulder.

"Boots."

"Excellent boots."

Mabel said, "Story people always liked this place because water hides here."

"Hides?"

"Not magic. Ground's full of old gravel under mud. Water moves sideways. My father called it hiding because he was better with children than geology."

That was exactly what I wanted.

Story language preserving observation without being technical report.

"Does White Reed feed Morrow?"

"Some years. Some ways. Ask district if you want percentages they don't have."

"Does Mirror Spring feed this basin?"

"Partly. Probably. There are side seeps. Rain shelf. Fog drip. Old stone channels nobody maps because half collapsed before my mother was born."

Messier.

Better.

"So strong Mirror sample doesn't prove strong Morrow supply."

"Of course not."

Jonah sighed.

"Can anybody in this district answer one thing with one thing?"

Mabel looked at him.

"Water doesn't."

That landed.

We ate early bread outside keeper house.

Jonah had to turn back soon if he wanted graft house by second ring plus lateness margin.

He kept looking at old path.

"I remember Sleeping Stone," he said.

"You were twelve."

"I know."

"You don't have to prove you remember."

"I know."

He sounded like he did not know emotionally.

Mabel cleaned mud rod.

"Your grandfather took you once."

"Twice."

"Second time you cried because thorn beetle got in your shirt."

"I was twelve."

"You said that already."

Jonah looked at me.

"I could take you farther."

There.

Temptation.

Local memory.

Extra body.

Company.

And his actual life waiting downhill.

"No."

"Sleeping Stone is only maybe two bells."

"Maybe."

"I know first climb."

"You have work."

"Grandfather said he'd cover half."

"If White Reed runs long. Not if you convert yourself into expedition staff."

"That's not your decision."

True.

I stopped.

"No. It's yours."

He looked surprised.

Good correction.

"My contract does not require you. Your family needs you. Your placement matters. If you decide to come anyway, I'm not going to pretend I own you. But I am not asking."

He stared up path.

Long time.

"Mom will be furious."

"Relevant."

"Grandfather will be pleased."

"Suspiciously relevant."

"Graft master will dock me."

"Very relevant."

He kicked pale reed stalk.

Mabel said, "Don't kick my reeds because adulthood is disappointing."

Jonah stopped.

Then laughed despite himself.

"I'm going back."

"Good," Mabel said.

He frowned at her.

"You could let me feel noble for one second."

"No."

He gave me his route memory anyway.

After White Reed, stay uphill of old stone channel until split oak.

At split oak, left path goes abandoned charcoal cut.

Right follows dark slab ridge.

Sleeping Stone visible only after last turn.

There was once a marker pile at split.

Maybe gone.

"If you hit two burned stumps, wrong way," Jonah said.

"How old are those instructions?"

"Nine years."

"Wonderful."

"The stumps were already old."

"Improving rapidly."

Mabel added current information.

"Split oak still standing last spring. Charcoal cut washed deeper. Don't take it. I haven't walked beyond first rise this year."

Current enough.

I copied separately:

**Jonah memory, age 12 / nine years old: uphill of stone channel; split oak; right toward slab ridge; old marker pile uncertain; burned stumps indicate charcoal cut/wrong route.**

**Mabel current-ish: split oak standing last spring; charcoal cut more washed; first rise only this year; no condition knowledge beyond.**

Different evidence ages.

Good.

Jonah handed me his flask.

"Keep it."

"You said return at White Reed."

"Mabel has water."

"You need one going back."

"I can fill here."

Mabel said, "He can. Take flask."

Useful ordinary loan.

Not magical gear.

"I'll return it."

Jonah shrugged.

"If you don't, Grandfather will add it to contract somehow."

Then he left.

Actually left.

Downhill.

No dramatic turn at path.

No last-minute party formation.

Within five minutes orchard trees swallowed him.

Mabel watched me watching.

"You nervous?"

"Yes."

"Good."

"Everybody here likes that answer."

"People who say no before old road are either lying or young."

"I have been both."

She pointed at pack.

"Too heavy?"

"Manageable."

"That's not answer."

Apparently disease spread.

I adjusted shoulder strap.

"Heavy enough uphill will matter. Not heavy enough to leave required gear."

"Better."

I checked crutch tips.

Wrap.

Straps.

Water.

Bottle case secure high in pack.

Trial limb wrapped.

Weather.

Fog mostly lifted.

East wind beginning in reed tops.

Dry forecast still.

Old path entrance was narrow dirt line above basin.

First twenty paces easy.

Then roots.

Then slope.

I stopped after maybe fifty.

Looked back.

Mabel keeper house below.

White Reed silver around water.

Maintained bridge.

Farm road.

Orchards beyond.

Could still turn around and lose nothing but dignity, which had never been expensive.

I continued.

The first rise was harder than map implied.

Maps rarely included shoulders.

Crutches bit dirt well where dry.

Roots created step problems.

I used old technique.

Not magic.

Not S-class anything.

Three points.

Set.

Move.

Breathe before needed.

Don't wait for fatigue to announce itself.

Rest on schedule rather than pride.

Pack changed balance backward.

I shortened stride.

At one steep patch I sat and scooted three body lengths rather than pretend standing method superior.

Adventure dignity remained cheap.

At top of first rise, Mabel was no longer visible.

Only reed basin through branches.

Isolation arrived quietly.

No music.

No monster.

Just nobody close enough to hear normal voice.

I sat on flat stone and drank.

Heart working.

Arms warm.

Right shoulder fine.

Left residual limb fine because no socket.

Crutch palms beginning pressure but normal.

Good.

Old stone channel appeared beside path.

Half buried.

Not carrying surface water.

Moss inside.

Damp at bottom.

Story path and water path overlapping again.

I did not assume connection to Mirror.

I wrote.

Then heard stone click uphill.

One click.

Pause.

Another.

Not falling continuously.

Movement.

I froze.

Old me came closer.

Not locked-in fully.

Just attention changing shape.

Wind east.

Leaves moving right-to-left.

Click came against wind side.

Something stepping.

Could be person.

Could be animal.

I moved off center of path to wider patch beside channel wall.

Back protected by stone enough.

Crutches planted.

No stupid weapon improvisation.

Wait.

A long-bodied animal came through brush uphill.

Not monster.

Deer-sized maybe smaller.

Six legs.

Gray-brown hide.

Flat antler-like plates sweeping backward from skull.

It stopped when it saw me.

I stopped harder.

We regarded each other.

I knew broad category from first life.

Shelf grazer.

Not exact local species.

Usually flight animal.

Usually mattered.

Its left rear leg was caught in trailing vine? No. Thin old wire.

Ah.

Old route infrastructure again.

Wire loop dragged from leg into brush.

Animal shifted.

Wire tightened.

It jerked.

Bad.

I was not touching that.

Could maybe cut wire if animal restrained.

Animal was not restrained in useful sense.

It had six legs and several opinions.

I backed one crutch-length.

Slow.

Grazer lowered head.

Plates forward enough to make geometry obvious.

Not attack yet.

Pressure.

I gave path.

More space downhill.

Animal pulled uphill.

Wire caught branch.

It panicked.

Now action.

It lunged sideways, not at me exactly, but path was narrow and I occupied part of available world.

Old me calculated before current body finished objecting.

Do not retreat straight downhill on crutches.

Drop center.

I sat hard behind channel wall.

Not graceful.

Grazer crossed where chest had been, plates clipping leaves.

Wire snapped branch.

Animal stumbled, recovered on too many legs, dragged loose wire uphill, disappeared.

Silence.

My heart upgraded contract price without authorization.

I stayed seated.

Count.

Hands.

Elbows.

Hip.

Residual limb.

Pack.

Bottle.

All fine.

One crutch had fallen across path.

Retrieved from seated position.

No chase.

No rescue attempt.

No magic.

I inspected where wire had caught.

Old copper line, green-black with age, partly buried along channel wall.

Maybe old signal? Water marker? Fence? Unknown.

Animal had pulled several lengths free.

I did not follow.

I marked hazard:

**First rise above White Reed: old thin wire exposed along path/channel edge, partly buried, now pulled loose by local six-legged shelf grazer. Entanglement hazard. Animal departed uphill with some wire trailing. Do not assume wire function.**

Useful route note.

Also:

**Path narrow enough animal passage required yielding. Wider refuge at channel-wall break approx. first rise.**

That was adventure.

Not glorious.

I preferred glorious in retrospect anyway.

I waited another few minutes.

Then stood carefully.

The path continued uphill toward split oak.

Sleeping Stone somewhere beyond.

White Reed no longer visible.

Jonah already headed back to work.

Mabel below keeping water holes.

Garrick waiting for evidence.

Helena waiting for me not to be stupid.

Twelve copper waiting at theoretical successful end.

And somewhere above, water had gone somewhere.

I adjusted pack.

Set crutches.

Continued.

Trailblazer was generous title.

The trail already existed.

Mostly.

My job was to find out how much of it still did.

# CHAPTER 461

## THE SURVIVOR

The split oak was dead.

That was inconvenient mostly because Jonah had described it as difficult to miss.

Alive trees had leaves.

Dead trees had opinions.

This one had both trunks split wide over the old channel, one half gray and bare, the other carrying three stubborn branches of yellowing leaves.

Good enough.

I stopped under it and checked route copy.

White Reed behind.

Sleeping Stone ahead.

Charcoal cut left after split oak.

Washed out.

Avoid.

Old water wall should stay on right until path climbs away.

I looked.

One branch left dropped into darker trees.

One continued uphill beside broken channel stone.

Right wall.

Take right.

Simple.

I hated simple.

The old path had narrowed enough that crutch placement became constant work.

Root.

Stone.

Dry dirt.

Another stone.

Left crutch tip on flat.

Right foot through.

Pack settle.

Repeat.

No trial leg.

Wrong terrain.

My shoulders had started complaining before my lungs did.

Current body knew the route was uphill even if old brain wanted to call it moderate.

I took a drink from Jonah's flask.

Small.

Water supply mattered now.

The old channel beside trail kept changing character.

At White Reed it had been damp and mossed.

Here it was mostly dry.

Then fifty paces later, wet again.

Not flowing.

Just black stone with tiny beads of moisture in cracks.

Story said Sleeping Stone had moss on downhill face.

That didn't mean anything yet.

Everything shaded had moss.

I wrote:

**Above split oak. Old channel intermittently damp. No continuous surface flow. No claim source connection.**

Then kept climbing.

The charcoal branch appeared exactly where Mabel said.

Almost.

The problem was somebody had stacked three small stones at its mouth.

Not old marker stones.

Too clean.

Recent enough edges hadn't weathered.

Human trace.

I stopped.

No name needed.

No person visible.

Charcoal branch descended left through washed dirt.

Fresh boot prints?

Maybe.

Two partial depressions.

Could be worker.

Could be Jonah-sized memory invented by wish.

Don't overclaim.

I marked:

**Three-stone stack at charcoal branch mouth. Appears deliberate/recent relative to surrounding weathering. Two possible tread impressions, uncertain.**

Could mean someone had marked danger.

Could mean lunch.

Could mean absolutely nothing.

I stayed right.

Good.

Path climbed around shoulder.

Wind changed.

East wind.

Later than weather board predicted? Maybe.

Not useful.

Cooler.

Leaves turned silver underside.

I could hear water now.

That stopped me.

Not channel beside me.

Farther uphill.

Thin rush.

Intermittent.

Then gone when wind moved.

Could be stream.

Could be trees.

Could be old brain wanting quest soundtrack.

I listened another half minute.

Nothing.

Moved.

The trail leveled briefly onto narrow shelf.

Dry.

Flat enough I considered trial limb.

Then looked ahead.

Shelf lasted maybe forty paces before dropping into roots and broken stone again.

Not worth fitting.

Crutches stayed.

At far end, route vanished.

Not metaphorically.

The old channel wall continued.

Path did not.

A wedge of earth and brush had slid down from uphill sometime recently enough that roots still showed pale where torn.

Not huge.

Maybe twenty feet across.

Enough to erase trail.

There was room below channel wall, but slope fell steep into trees.

Above slide, ground looked rough but possible.

I stopped well back.

This was not Three Sisters.

No contract turnaround yet.

Just route problem.

I took off pack.

Set it behind stable stone.

No point making balance worse while scouting three paces.

Crutches planted.

I tested edge with right foot.

Firm.

One pace.

Another.

Slide material mostly dry crumb and root mat.

No standing water.

Across it, old wall visible again.

Could traverse high edge where roots remained anchored.

Maybe.

Could descend around below.

Bad with crutches.

Could climb above and rejoin.

Steeper but tree roots.

Old me ranked options automatically.

Not because genius.

Because I had spent decades deciding which bad ground would kill me slower.

High route.

But before committing, I heard the sound again.

Water.

Closer.

A hollow knock under dirt.

Then another.

Not water exactly.

Stone taking load.

I froze.

The slide face gave tiny twitch.

Loose dirt ran down two inches.

Oh.

Not stable.

Back.

I shifted weight onto crutches.

One step.

Then the uphill root mat tore.

Not dramatic at first.

A crack.

Brush jerked.

Then half the slide started moving.

Action became simple.

Not fast.

Correct.

I could not run.

So don't try.

Pack behind me.

Stable wall to right.

Channel wall knee-high.

Big root clump coming down left-center.

Two fist-sized stones bouncing first.

I dropped my center.

Right crutch forward.

Left crutch folded close.

Turn sideways behind wall.

A stone skipped past where my shin had been.

Dirt hit boots.

Root clump struck edge of old channel wall and broke apart.

Then something larger moved above.

Flat slab.

About door-sized.

Sliding, not rolling.

Slow enough to see.

Fast enough to matter.

Its path would hit the wall section I was using.

No time to climb.

No room downhill.

Could retreat three paces to thicker wall if I moved now.

Current body said three paces on crutches through sliding dirt was expensive.

Old brain said staying cost more.

Move.

Right foot.

Plant.

Crutch.

Drag pack strap? Pack still behind stable stone farther back, not on me. Good.

Second step.

Dirt shifted under right foot.

I went low, almost knee.

Left stump safely clear inside wrap? No trial leg. Residual limb above ground.

Crutch tip slid.

Locked-in mode arrived without permission.

Sound narrowed.

Stone.

Dirt.

My breath.

Slab scrape.

Distance.

Angle.

I had done this with two legs once.

Body did not care about nostalgia.

I shoved through right arm and intact leg.

Third pace.

Behind thicker wall.

Slab hit.

Old channel exploded sideways in dust and broken black stone.

A fragment came over top.

Head-sized.

Too late to duck fully.

Magic.

Not test.

Need.

I pushed.

No shaping sequence.

No careful breath count.

Just old command into current weak channel.

Away.

The fragment kicked sideways.

Not far.

Enough.

It clipped my pack-free shoulder instead of face and struck dirt behind me.

Pain.

Sharp.

Not catastrophic.

I was already down.

More earth came.

I curled behind wall and covered head.

Crutches tucked.

Wait.

Stone hissed.

Leaves snapped.

Then less.

Then only pebbles.

Then quiet.

I counted five breaths.

Ten.

Locked-in stayed too long.

Everything wanted action.

No.

Stay.

Listen.

Nothing moving uphill.

No second crack.

Shoulder hurt.

Right hand working.

Left arm working.

Neck fine.

Head fine.

Chest fine.

Right leg fine.

Residual limb untouched.

Crutches?

Right crutch under elbow.

Left beside me.

I checked shafts.

No visible crack.

Tips intact.

Good.

I sat another full minute.

Then laughed once.

Bad sign.

"Adventure," I told dirt.

Dirt declined comment.

I stood carefully.

Shoulder pain deepened when I lifted arm overhead.

Could still bear crutch load?

Test gently.

Yes.

Painful but functional.

Not smart to pretend nothing.

I stepped back toward pack.

Pack had taken dust but stayed behind original stable stone.

Sample bottle inside padded wrap.

Check.

Seal empty intact.

Good.

Food intact.

Trial limb wrap dusty, not damaged.

Good.

I sat and wrote before memory became heroic.

**Route loss above split oak, before Sleeping Stone. Recent/active earth slide approximately twenty feet across old trail. While scouting edge, upper root mat released and slide moved again. Old channel wall section struck by large flat slab and partially collapsed. I retreated behind thicker wall. One stone fragment deflected and clipped right shoulder. No head strike. Crutches intact. Sample bottle intact. Shoulder painful but load-bearing. Trail crossing not attempted after release.**

Then magic line.

This mattered.

**External attempt/use 139: emergency deflection, no setup, stone fragment at close range. Visible lateral change sufficient to alter strike from face/head line to shoulder line. No measurement. No repeat. High-stress context.**

Protocol.

59 minimal.

53 shaping.

139 external.

That was a real result.

Ugly.

Useful.

Expensive.

My head buzzed slightly from locked-in focus more than magic.

Temper followed.

I was angry at slope.

At old path.

At Garrick Sol for saying day up like calendar owed him terrain.

At myself for scouting too close after hearing load noise.

That one deserved anger.

I had heard warning.

I had still stepped onto edge.

Old experience was not permission to be clever.

Good lesson.

Now route.

Trail erased.

Slide active.

Could turn back.

Contract guaranteed nothing below Three Sisters.

But safety mattered more.

Could also find bypass without crossing slide face.

I was before Sleeping Stone.

Old channel likely continued around shoulder.

Topographic option uphill through trees.

Need assess without entering unstable zone.

I moved back twenty paces to where slope above was intact.

Looked uphill.

Trees spaced enough.

Ground steep but rooted.

Could climb around slide crown and descend to trail beyond.

Shoulder pain made crutch climbing worse.

Could wait.

Could rest and see if pain settled.

I did.

Half bell maybe.

Food.

Water.

Cold cloth from flask on shoulder.

No swelling obvious through shirt.

Tender outer shoulder.

Could lift arm to chest height comfortably.

Overhead hurt.

Crutch loading used downward push mostly.

Manageable.

Turnaround threshold wasn't only broken bone.

Three-day quest did not require turning one bruise into damage.

I considered returning White Reed.

Honestly.

Not because cowardice.

Because route had just tried to bury me before first shelter.

Then I looked uphill bypass.

Stable root network.

No exposed slide scar until far side.

Could take slow.

Could stop if shoulder worsened.

No need to cross active debris.

Proceed.

Not prove.

Proceed conditionally.

I repacked.

Tightened shoulder strap differently so pack sat more on left side/waist harness.

Crutches.

Climb.

Ugly.

Ten paces felt like fifty.

I used roots as handholds only after testing.

Sometimes sat and moved pack first.

No dignity.

Excellent survival method.

At top of bypass, I found something interesting.

Old cut stone.

Not channel wall.

Two squared blocks sunk into hillside with iron loops rusted almost gone.

Anchoring point maybe.

Old maintenance line?

Could relate to exposed wire below.

Do not invent.

I wrote:

**Two squared anchor blocks above slide crown, iron loops heavily rusted. Possible old route/channel maintenance infrastructure. Function unknown.**

No conspiracy.

No treasure.

I kept moving.

Far side descent easier.

Trail reappeared beside channel after maybe hundred paces.

I sat.

Shoulder still functional.

No worse.

Good.

Then I noticed channel.

Water.

Actual surface water now.

Thin ribbon.

Not enough to cover bottom.

Running downhill.

I stared.

Had slide exposed it?

Could be seep from disturbed slope.

Could be local branch entering above.

Could be Mirror system.

No claim.

I marked exact location relative to slide and tasted nothing because I wasn't idiot.

District sample bottle remained for source.

My normal flask still had enough.

Continue.

Path curved.

Trees opened.

And there it was.

Sleeping Stone.

Story name was annoyingly accurate.

Long black slab jutted from hillside over shallow recess, rounded top like shoulder under blanket.

Downhill face covered in moss.

Of course.

But not evenly.

Upper half dry gray.

Lower third dark green.

Under overhang, old soot stained stone.

Human shelter use.

Recent?

Maybe.

There was also a small stack of cut orchard twine in back corner.

Not ancient.

Definitely not nine years old.

Somebody had used shelter more recently than Garrick's memory suggested.

No person.

No name.

I checked ground.

One cold fire ring.

Ash old enough dry.

Boot marks too confused to date.

Twine cleaner than rest.

I wrote.

Then sat under stone.

Shelter.

Finally.

I unwrapped shoulder enough to inspect.

Bruise already forming.

No deformity.

Movement limited by pain but present.

Could continue tomorrow if no overnight worsening.

Today?

No.

Enough.

Quest had given me route slide, shoulder strike, bypass climb, emergency magic, and Sleeping Stone.

I had earned stopping.

I set blanket.

Food.

Water.

No fire yet.

East wind reached overhang but weak.

Story said dry unless east wind.

Story got that part partly right too.

Not prophecy.

Observation preserved.

I looked at moss.

Downhill face green.

The old story had called it sign.

But moss here made sense from shade and seep.

There was a wet line behind stone.

Tiny.

Coming from uphill crack.

Not source proof.

Still water sign.

I wrote:

**Sleeping Stone reached. Downhill/lower face mossed; upper face dry. Small seep from uphill crack behind overhang. Story-memory matches a real persistent wet micro-site, but cause/local connection unknown.**

Good.

Then the action replayed in my head.

Fragment.

Push.

Shoulder.

Old brain had not asked permission.

That was interesting.

External 139 had worked under ugly conditions better than some clean tests.

Maybe because need narrowed intent.

Maybe because adrenaline changed output.

Maybe because fragment already moving and tiny lateral change required less than moving static object.

Maybe because I got lucky.

All valid.

Do not build theory tonight.

I wrote all four possibilities.

Then stopped.

No second test.

No celebratory pebble pushing under shelter.

The magic had done its job.

Let it be useful.

Dark came early under trees.

No signal from Morrow.

No Jonah.

No keeper.

No road bells.

For first time this trip, I was sleeping somewhere nobody had sold me floor space.

That felt more like adventure than getting nearly flattened.

Barely.

I checked route copy.

Sleeping Stone.

Next:

Three Sisters Hollow.

Landslide country.

Wonderful.

My shoulder throbbed.

I put notebook away.

Survivor.

Bit dramatic.

I had survived a small slide, not war.

But titles had been generous before.

And old me knew survival was usually less impressive from inside it.

Mostly it looked like hearing the wrong sound, moving before pride finished talking, and sitting down when sitting down was the smartest thing available.

I lay under Sleeping Stone listening to the tiny seep tick into dirt.

Tomorrow I would decide whether my shoulder still belonged to the expedition.

Tonight it did.

That was enough.

# CHAPTER 462

## THE PATHFINDER

My shoulder had improved overnight.

Which was not same as good.

I learned this by reaching for blanket corner with right hand and immediately reconsidering optimism as philosophy.

Sleeping Stone held gray morning.

East wind had pushed mist under overhang sometime before dawn. Not rain. Fine droplets on outer blanket edge and black stone lip.

I sat up slowly.

Neck fine.

Head fine.

Shoulder bruised dark across upper front, tender where stone had clipped me.

Arm forward to chest height.

Fine enough.

Out to side.

Fine enough until higher.

Overhead.

No.

Crutch load test seated first.

Plant right crutch.

Press gradually.

Ache.

Stable.

More pressure.

Still stable.

No sharp catch.

Good.

Standing load next.

Ugly but functional.

Yesterday's stone had purchased itself one morning of attention.

Fair.

Three Sisters remained ahead.

White Reed behind involved slide bypass, downhill travel, and admitting route had beaten me before contractual threshold.

That was not reason to continue.

Important.

Actual reasons:

shoulder functional;

weather dry enough;

food sufficient;

water sufficient;

route evidence still plausible;

Three Sisters was next major decision point.

Continue conditionally.

I ate flatbread and dried fruit.

Refilled ordinary flask from Sleeping Stone seep?

No.

Unknown quality.

I had enough water from White Reed plus Jonah's flask.

Do not create stomach adventure.

I recorded seep unchanged from night observation and packed.

District bottle remained sealed.

Trial limb remained wrapped.

Shoulder made extra carried weight more annoying, but rough route still made crutches safer than experimental gait.

I adjusted pack so blanket rode lower and trial build closer to spine.

Small difference.

Useful.

Before leaving, I looked again at orchard twine in shelter corner.

Cut clean.

Not ancient.

Could have been last month.

Could have been last year.

Could have been five years if dry enough.

I wrote:

**Sleeping Stone: human use later than original abandonment possible. Twine condition suggests not ancient. Timing unknown.**

No mystery stranger.

Just people using a place people supposedly stopped using.

Humans were inconsiderate toward clean historical narratives.

I left after first light.

Above Sleeping Stone, path did something stories rarely mentioned.

It became boring.

For almost a bell.

Thank gods.

Old channel climbed gently along hillside.

Trees thinner.

Ground dry under pale grass.

Occasional stone edging still visible.

My shoulder warmed with crutch use but didn't worsen.

I shortened stride.

More rests.

Not because route demanded drama.

Because body demanded arithmetic.

At first rest I checked map.

Sleeping Stone to Three Sisters had one useful line on Garrick's copy:

**keep water wall until wall turns inward; cross hollow where sisters show together.**

Story version Jonah remembered:

**When three sisters share one shadow, leave the old water and walk between them.**

Same instruction?

Maybe.

Different enough to matter.

The water wall continued.

Then stopped.

Not gradually.

One section simply ended at a broad fan of pale gravel.

Beyond it, hillside opened into shallow basin with three separate ridges visible through morning haze.

No three stone spires.

I stopped.

Map problem.

Could be wrong basin.

Could be Sisters not visible from this angle.

Could be haze.

Could be landmark changed.

Could be I had already drifted.

I backed to last certain point.

Important habit.

Last certain point: old channel wall, one large white-barked tree growing through outer stones, water wall curving slightly inward.

Garrick: wall turns inward.

Here.

So likely hollow.

Story: three sisters share one shadow.

No sisters.

I waited for haze to move.

It didn't care.

Could use sun? Morning light behind ridge. Shadow language might refer time of day, not route geometry.

Unhelpful now.

I scanned ground.

Two possible tracks.

Lower track followed gravel fan east.

Upper track crossed grass toward low saddle.

No current marker.

Old route copy showed channel line ending before Three Sisters, then single line through hollow.

Which line?

No scale.

Excellent.

I sat.

This was pathfinding.

Not walking where somebody already knew.

Evidence.

Lower gravel had wash channels.

Recent enough to cut grass edge.

Upper track had compacted soil patches but could be animals.

One old squared stone lay half buried near upper track.

Human infrastructure clue.

Not proof path.

I checked back side.

Shallow groove across top.

Could have held something.

Water infrastructure maybe.

Could also be old boundary marker.

I left it.

Then saw three black points appear when haze shifted.

Not ahead.

Farther north.

Three narrow stone spires on ridge shoulder.

Three Sisters.

From where I sat they were separate.

As sun cleared cloud edge, their shadows stretched downslope.

Not one shadow.

Story instruction likely depended time or position.

Garrick's practical instruction better:

cross hollow where sisters show together.

They showed together from upper track.

Lower gravel route angled away.

Upper.

I marked decision.

No triumph.

Just less wrong.

The upper track climbed toward saddle.

Shoulder objected.

I rested every short rise.

At one point I moved pack ten paces ahead, returned, then moved myself.

Slow.

Ridiculous.

Effective.

By midday Three Sisters filled skyline.

They weren't sisters up close.

They were three narrow basalt fins rising from broken ridge, each perhaps several stories tall, with different lean.

From below, two overlapped.

Third stood apart.

Hollow beneath them was broad bowl of grass, exposed stone, and old slide scars.

And there it was.

Lower bench.

Or what remained.

I stopped well back.

Contract threshold.

Three Sisters reached.

Four copper now possible if route beyond genuinely unsafe and notes useful.

That should not matter to safety judgment.

Which meant I needed to notice that it mattered.

I did.

Then put money away mentally.

Assess.

Old lower bench crossed left side of hollow beneath fins.

First third intact.

Middle section gone.

Not `narrow.`

Gone.

A slide scar dropped through it into ravine below.

Opposite bench resumed perhaps thirty paces across.

Loose north-face upper track visible as faint line climbing above scar.

Explicit no-go.

Garrick had said don't climb it if lower bench cut.

Lower bench was cut.

Contract answer looked simple.

Turn around.

Four copper.

Useful notes.

Quest fails safely.

I should have felt disappointed.

Instead I felt suspicious.

Not of Garrick.

Of word `route.`

Old path gone did not mean no path.

But contract had explicit warning because people died by inventing alternatives.

Do not become clever corpse.

I stayed back and surveyed hollow without approaching scar.

Three Sisters bowl had old water wall descending from right.

Not same wall as below? Maybe continuation.

A shallow seasonal channel cut through center grass.

Currently damp.

No meaningful flow.

At far southern side, below slide scar, trees thickened along contour.

Could be lower bypass.

Could be cliff.

Could be nothing.

Map did not show.

I moved along safe inner hollow, not toward slide edge.

At southern end found old stone steps.

Three.

Then five.

Then a descending line hidden under fern.

Not trail exactly.

Constructed.

Old.

Where go?

Down.

Potential bypass beneath scar.

Garrick had warned against north-face upper track specifically.

He had not mentioned southern descent.

That did not make it safe.

But it made it information.

I followed only until first turn, using crutches carefully.

Shoulder now more than ache.

Still stable.

Steps descended to narrow shelf under trees.

Shelf continued east beneath slide scar, hidden from hollow.

Old retaining stones.

Recent cut branches.

There.

Human use again.

Not ancient.

Somebody had maintained enough to cut branches.

I stopped.

Could be current route.

Could be hunter path.

Could terminate under unstable scar.

Need inspect without committing.

I left pack at top? No, never separate sample/gear too far. I had pack. Fine.

I descended another short section.

Shelf widened.

Then sound.

Water.

Not trickle.

Running water below.

I moved to safe opening between trees.

A narrow stream ran east under slide debris, emerging from broken stone culvert beneath lower bench line.

Interesting.

Old route's water had not vanished.

At least some water crossed beneath scar.

No source claim.

Could be local tributary.

Could be Mirror system.

I recorded.

Then noticed something else.

A plank.

Across stream.

Weathered but not rotten-looking from distance.

One end rested on old stone.

Other on newer stacked rocks.

Human crossing.

Recent enough arrangement maybe.

No footprints clear.

No person.

This changed decision.

Not `safe route exists.`

Only `somebody has used a lower crossing after bench failure.`

I could inspect.

Should I?

Shoulder.

Time.

Weather.

Contract.

Three days food.

Mirror Spring likely beyond.

If lower bypass crossed beneath scar and rejoined bench, I could continue without violating explicit north-face no-go.

But walking directly under active slide scar would be stupid.

Was shelf under scar or below stable toe?

Need geometry.

I backed up to hollow and looked from distance.

Southern descent curved below scar's lower toe, not beneath vertical release face.

Trees on shelf mature.

Retaining wall old.

Likely older alternate water-maintenance route.

Still risk.

Not same as upper loose track.

I ate.

Rested shoulder.

Checked sky.

East wind increasing.

No rain.

Haze thinning.

Three Sisters shadows now stretched differently.

At this angle, all three shadows touched across hollow floor.

Shared one shadow.

I laughed.

"Of course."

Story instruction wasn't telling me where route began.

It might be telling time.

When sun reached certain angle, shadows connected.

And where they connected?

The joined shadow crossed southern descent mouth.

That was too neat.

Dangerous neat.

I did not let it become proof.

But people without clocks could encode departure timing or route visibility in story.

Maybe.

Or coincidence.

Write both.

**At current season/day position, three spire shadows join across hollow near southern descent after midday. Story phrase may encode timing/position. Not established.**

This was why stories survived.

Not because they were precise.

Because sometimes precision wore badly and image wore well.

Decision.

I could stop here.

Contract would pay four.

Safe.

Useful.

Or inspect lower bypass far enough to determine whether it rejoined old route without entering unstable toe.

Shoulder functional but accumulating pain.

I had daylight.

Weather dry.

Food.

No companion.

That last one cut both ways.

Nobody to help.

Nobody to pressure.

I set turnaround:

one bell maximum;

no travel beneath fresh bare scar;

no loose slope;

no jump/gap requiring crutch commitment;

if shelf narrows below safe crutch placement, return;

if crossing plank questionable, do not use;

if shoulder worsens sharply, return to Sleeping Stone.

Good.

I descended southern steps again.

At stream crossing, plank looked worse close.

One side cracked.

No.

But stream itself narrow.

Maybe three paces.

Water below knee depth? Hard to tell. Fast enough to matter.

Stones.

Crutches and current.

No.

Could cross upstream where wider/shallow?

I followed bank without committing.

Thirty paces south, stream spread over flat stone into broad ankle-deep sheet.

Better.

Bottom visible.

Still slippery.

Could use trial limb?

Absolutely not.

Crutches.

Need three points.

Shoulder.

This was actual decision.

I tested first crutch tip on submerged stone from bank.

Held.

Second.

Held.

Step right foot.

Water cold.

Left side absent lower leg meant body geometry strange but familiar.

Crutch placement wide.

Move.

One.

Pause.

Two.

Right shoulder complained under load.

Not sharp.

Three.

Halfway.

Water pushed crutch shaft, not enough to move tip.

Then upstream pulse.

Not flood.

A sudden stronger surge, maybe release from pocket or gate? No gate here known.

Water rose around right ankle and hit both crutches harder.

I froze.

Bad.

Moving during unknown surge worse.

Plant.

Lower center.

Wait.

Surge lasted seconds.

Then eased.

No magic.

No need.

I backed out.

Same route.

Slow.

Returned south bank.

Decision made.

Not today.

Could perhaps cross after water settled, but shoulder already had one warning.

And unknown pulses made shallow ford less predictable.

Useful information.

I recorded time, weather, approximate water rise, no claim cause.

Three Sisters lower bench cut.

North-face upper track no-go.

Southern old stone descent exists and shows recent branch cutting.

Lower shelf reaches stream crossing below slide toe.

Plank cracked/untrusted.

Shallow alternate ford exists south but experienced short unpredicted surge during test; aborted crossing.

That was enough.

Contract threshold met.

Four copper likely.

But Mirror Spring remained beyond.

Could I camp Three Sisters and try morning lower water?

Three-day food.

Day one Morrow to Sleeping Stone.

Day two Sleeping Stone to Three Sisters.

If camp here, day three would be attempt beyond plus return impossible to Morrow same day likely.

Food nominal three days, not cliff edge.

Could ration.

But shoulder.

And sample return.

Adventure had finally reached the point where continuing required changing plan, not merely walking harder.

Good.

I climbed back into hollow.

Needed shelter.

Three Sisters had no named shelter on map.

Sleeping Stone behind maybe one bell plus shoulder, more downhill than uphill? Could return there safely before dark if I left now.

That preserved option to turn back White Reed tomorrow.

Or maybe try Three Sisters again morning from Sleeping Stone? Waste distance.

Better to find safe hollow camp only if weather dry.

East wind.

No rain watch.

But exposed hollow.

I inspected southern wall and found shallow rock lip, not Sleeping Stone quality but enough windbreak.

Ground dry.

No overhead loose rock.

Camp.

This was risk but reasonable.

I chose it.

No fire.

Food.

Water.

Shoulder rest.

I checked bruise.

Darker.

No new swelling obvious.

Crutch load after rest still stable.

Good.

Trial limb stayed wrapped.

District bottle stayed sealed.

Mirror Spring remained unsampled.

Magic stayed 139.

No reason to make water surge about me.

At dusk, Three Sisters became black against pale sky.

Their shadows disappeared because everything became shadow.

Story less useful then.

I wrote route notes carefully.

Not because contract.

Because if I turned back, notes were what made failure useful.

Then I noticed one detail I had missed.

The recent branch cuts on southern descent.

Cuts were angled clean.

Not weather break.

At least several.

Some leaves on cut twigs were still dull green.

Recent.

How recent?

Days maybe.

Weeks maybe depending plant.

Not years.

Someone had used or cleared lower bypass recently.

That did not mean safe.

But it meant tomorrow's choice wasn't between known dead route and stupid improvisation.

There was current human behavior to understand.

No person appeared.

No camp smoke.

No convenient guide.

Just cuts.

I added:

**Southern descent vegetation cut recently enough leaves remain partly green. Identity/purpose unknown. Do not infer route safety.**

Then one more line:

**Question tomorrow: observe stream before first ring. If baseline lower/stable and shoulder improved, consider bounded crossing attempt only if return margin remains credible. Otherwise turn back and claim Three Sisters threshold.**

Pathfinder.

Not because I found path.

Because I found where certainty ended and still had to decide what counted as a road.

Three Sisters stood above me.

Mirror Spring somewhere beyond.

For first time since accepting contract, I could earn money by going home.

That made continuing more honest.

Tomorrow I would find out whether honest was enough.

# CHAPTER 463

## THE CROSSER

The stream was lower in the morning.

Not dramatically.

Enough to matter.

I stood above southern descent before first ring with shoulder already warm from packing and watched water move over the flat stone ford.

Yesterday's line had reached around my ankle.

Now lower.

Yesterday's surprise pulse had pushed hard enough against both crutches to make retreat correct.

This morning I watched for one bell.

No pulse.

That did not mean none coming.

It meant one bell without one.

Better than zero bells.

Shoulder test first.

Right arm forward.

Side.

Load through crutch.

Ache.

No sharp change.

Bruise darker.

Range still limited overhead.

Functional.

Food count next.

Not enough for another cheerful three-day loop.

Enough for today and controlled return if I stopped pretending portions were suggestions.

I split remaining bread and cheese into travel and emergency.

Dried fruit already less than I wanted.

Water good.

Bottle empty.

Twelve copper.

Twenty-three Vale.

Four copper fallback waiting behind me if I returned with useful notes.

That mattered because now continuing was expensive in ways that had nothing to do with coin.

Time.

Food.

Shoulder.

Return distance.

And the increasingly irritating possibility that I might actually find what I came for.

I descended southern steps.

Recent branch cuts looked the same as last night.

Not useful.

At stream I ignored cracked plank.

Good decisions deserved repetition.

The shallow ford showed full stone bottom through clear water.

I walked bank upstream and down once.

No hidden deeper slot apparent.

No alternate dry crossing.

Current strongest toward center.

I set rules.

No trial limb.

Pack tightened high.

Bottle padded center.

One crutch moves at a time.

Right foot only after both crutches planted.

If water rises above yesterday's mark or shoulder sharpens, reverse immediately.

No pride adjustments halfway.

Then I entered.

First crutch.

Second.

Right foot.

Pause.

The water was cold enough to make concentration easy.

Second move.

Third.

Shoulder complained on right-side plant but held.

I shortened reach.

Less efficient.

More stable.

Center water pushed harder against crutch shafts.

No pulse.

Halfway.

I stopped.

Not because danger.

Because planned pause.

That distinction mattered.

Breathing normal.

Pack stable.

Bottle silent.

Move.

One.

Two.

Three.

Far bank close.

Then water increased.

Not yesterday's sudden shove.

A gradual rise.

Half finger around right boot.

Maybe normal variation.

Maybe beginning of pulse.

Decision.

Forward bank three placements.

Backward bank six.

Continue.

I moved faster without becoming sloppy.

Plant.

Plant.

Foot.

Plant.

Plant.

Foot.

Right crutch slipped half an inch on algae.

My body corrected before thought finished.

Left crutch loaded.

Hip low.

Right foot stayed planted.

Shoulder flashed pain.

No fall.

I reset tip farther apart.

One more sequence.

Far bank.

I sat on stone immediately.

No celebration.

Just inventory.

Shoulder worse for maybe thirty seconds.

Then settled back to angry baseline.

Crutches intact.

Pack dry above bottom seam.

Bottle intact.

Right boot soaked.

Crossing successful.

I recorded approximate water condition and that level rose slightly during crossing without abrupt pulse.

No cause claim.

No hero language.

Then I looked at route.

The southern shelf continued east under trees.

Recent cuts continued too.

One branch shaved chest high.

Another sapling bent and tied aside with plain fiber cord.

That was current use.

Not necessarily current today.

But intentional.

The shelf curved below Three Sisters scar, staying under mature trees and outside fresh bare slide face.

Old retaining wall held sections.

Other sections had become root shelf and dirt.

I moved slowly.

Shoulder made every right crutch plant a reminder.

About half bell later, route climbed.

Three old stone steps.

Then six.

Then the opposite intact lower bench appeared above.

Rejoin.

There it was.

Southern bypass connected across the cut.

Not safely proven for everyone.

Not maintained road.

But passable this morning for me under current conditions.

Useful.

I marked junction with notebook sketch.

No cairn.

No changing route.

Not my authority.

Opposite bench ran east with old water wall beside it.

Three Sisters receded behind.

Mirror Spring ahead somewhere beyond next ridge.

For first time I had crossed the contractual failure point.

Eight copper if I reached source and returned notes.

Twelve with sample.

Money surfaced in thought.

I disliked that.

Then accepted it.

Money was part of why I was here.

Pretending otherwise would be another kind of vanity.

The old bench climbed.

Not steep.

Relentless.

I rested often.

Shoulder more limiting than leg now.

Interesting reversal.

Trial build remained dead weight in pack.

Could have left it Morrow.

Maybe.

But future stable ground might justify carrying it.

Today did not.

Late morning I reached first strong human trace beyond Three Sisters.

Not person.

A low side channel had been cleared of leaves recently.

Too recently for years.

Mud along edge showed rectangular tool scrape.

Someone had maintained water movement here.

Why?

Could be orchard family.

Could be hunter using drainage.

Could be district.

Could be nobody connected to Morrow.

No names.

No conspiracy.

I wrote exact.

Then found something more useful.

A small wooden marker wedged under wall stone.

No words.

Three shallow cuts on one face.

One deep cut on other.

Could be route marker.

Could be maintenance tally.

Could be scrap.

Leave.

I was getting better at not turning objects into plot just because I found them.

The route narrowed under white cliff seam.

White.

There.

Garrick's description:

black-stone catch wall below white cliff seam.

Mirror Spring likely near.

I stopped.

Not because exhausted.

Because objective proximity changed behavior.

From now on, do not contaminate bottle.

Do not touch water systems casually.

Do not alter source.

Observe before approach.

The white seam ran across cliff face perhaps two hundred paces ahead.

Below it, trees thick.

I could hear water.

More than Three Sisters stream.

Steady.

Not roaring.

Enough.

Path descended slightly.

Shoulder liked downhill less because crutches took more braking load.

I sat twice and moved on rear rather than force it.

Old me would have mocked current me.

Old me also had two legs.

He could shut up.

Then I smelled something.

Wet stone.

Cold mineral.

And something sweet-green from moss.

No salt.

Still no salt.

Carrow felt absurdly far.

The path opened.

Mirror Spring.

Not magical mirror.

At least not visibly.

A broad black-stone catch wall sat under pale cliff seam.

Water emerged from three places.

One main spring mouth at center under stone lip.

Two thinner side seeps.

Catch basin below fed narrow channel running west and south through old stonework.

The basin surface reflected cliff in broken silver-black patches.

Mirror enough.

I did not move closer yet.

First observation from distance.

Main flow present.

Strong by eye compared to thin channels below.

But I did not know normal.

Side seep left active.

Right side weak.

Catch basin not empty.

No obvious giant root blocking mouth.

No story reenactment.

Good.

Then I saw why recent route cuts existed.

Someone had been here.

Recently.

Not person now.

Evidence.

Fresh boot or shoe impressions? Careful. Ground damp. Several tread-like marks near side wall, overlapping, not readable enough for count.

A coil of new fiber cord hung from peg under old catch roof.

One small hand shovel leaned against wall.

And one gate.

Not orchard-style major gate.

A narrow wooden diversion flap in secondary channel below basin.

Newer than surrounding stone.

Currently half closed.

That mattered.

I stayed still.

Contract explicitly said no altering gates.

Good rule.

Water left basin through two branches.

Main lower channel.

Secondary east-cut channel controlled partly by new flap.

Where did east branch go?

Unknown.

Could feed upper orchards.

Could feed maintenance pool.

Could be entirely unrelated to Morrow.

Do not accuse.

I approached basin edge on dry stone.

No trial limb.

Crutches careful.

Shoulder painful now.

I checked district form.

Fill point.

Observed conditions.

Seal.

Witness if available.

No witness.

Allowed blank if none? Form had witness if available according to Celia. Fine.

Need valid sample from source.

Where exactly counts source?

Main mouth or catch basin?

Celia had said fill point recorded. Bottle accepted supplemental, not authoritative.

Best minimally contaminated point without altering source.

Main outflow immediately below mouth accessible from flat stone.

Good.

Before opening bottle, inspect hands.

Dirty.

No washing with source then touching rim carelessly.

I had clean cloth wrap section.

Bottle instructions likely keep interior sterile by not touching lip/inside. Fine.

I unpadded bottle.

Seal intact.

Opened only when positioned.

Shoulder made two-handed handling awkward.

I sat.

Crutches beside.

Right foot braced.

Bottle low.

I filled from falling stream below main lip without touching stone or basin.

Water clear.

Not proof of anything.

Cap.

Seal strip.

Press.

Record.

**Mirror Spring main center outflow, below lip, no contact with basin stone. Main visible flow present. Two side seeps present, right weaker than left by visual observation only. Newer secondary diversion flap below catch basin half closed. No gate changes made. Recent human-use traces present. No witness available.**

Twelve copper condition maybe met if sample survived return and office accepted chain.

Not money yet.

Then I did something harder.

I looked at spring without wanting answer.

Why were lower Morrow channels low?

Spring visibly running.

That made one hypothesis less comfortable.

Not source dry.
