At least those component families belonged together historically.

I read line again.

Paired by stamped numbers.

Echo Step orphan disk at Lakeward had no pair.

Half-wheel frame had three slots and cloudy glass.

Maybe same family.

Not proof exact match.

But stronger.

"Instruction plate copied?"

"Partly. Corrosion ate most. Archive transcript says only fragments legible."

"Can I see transcript?"

"Lakeward archive."

Of course.

I had just left Lakeward.

Wonderful.

Then clerk added:

"Unless you want the ridge rubbing."

I looked at him.

"The what?"

"We have a charcoal rubbing from one legible line because old road supervisor thought it related to Marker Five."

"One bit?"

"No. That's on wall."

He pointed behind me.

I had walked past it twice.

Small framed paper.

Faded letters:

**... THIRD RETURN ... FACE WEST ... LIGHT ... HOME MARK ...**

Missing words.

Maybe instruction.

Maybe maintenance note.

Third return.

Face west.

Light.

Home mark.

Grandmother's version:

Don't trust Sunroad until your third shadow points home.

I stared longer.

Not proof.

But now story and damaged technical text touched each other.

That was different.

I copied exact visible fragment and gaps.

The clerk said, "Collectors love that one."

"Because it proves the saying?"

"Because they can make it prove whatever they're selling."

Excellent.

I closed notebook.

Tomorrow I had choices.

Return Lakeward and inspect archive transcript.

Stay Bracken and wait for ridge recovery of half-disk.

Walk Sunroad again and go beyond Seven.

Take Low Ridge Road toward Cinder Cross and keep moving east.

Four roads, effectively.

No contract.

No one waiting.

Sixteen copper became fifteen after bunk.

Five above floor.

Enough to choose.

Not enough to be stupid indefinitely.

I went back to bunkhouse.

Shoulder tired but not angry.

That was improvement.

I unwrapped trial limb only to inspect it.

Dry.

Healthy.

No wear today.

Magic stayed one hundred thirty-nine.

Lot Sixty-Seven stayed locked.

No purchases.

No artifact in my pocket.

Still, I had more than yesterday.

A real Three Rays road station.

A possible black route-disk fragment left where found.

A twenty-year inventory connecting frames, paired disks, and cloudy glass.

Three standing stones that flashed in sequence under current sun.

A damaged instruction fragment:

**third return**

**face west**

**light**

**home mark**

And an old saying that had changed shape between market and grandmother.

That was enough to make nine copper feel different.

Not cheap.

Not yet justified.

Different.

I lay back carefully so shoulder didn't complain.

The road had not given me treasure.

It had given me a better question.

Apparently that was becoming my preferred kind.

# CHAPTER 468

## THE EXPLORER

I went past Marker Seven because I wanted to.

That remained suspicious.

Bracken Vale was still asleep when I left.

Fifteen copper.

Twenty-three with Vale.

Five above floor.

Enough to explore badly exactly once.

I preferred exploring well.

The ridge office opened after first bell, so I could not ask anything new before departure.

That was fine.

I already had map.

Current notice.

Known hazards to Seven.

Past Seven, unknown.

I packed light again.

Water.

Food for day plus one emergency meal.

Notebook.

Repair wrap.

Line quietener.

Current reader.

HT-LP-4 tile.

No Lot Sixty-Seven.

No trial limb.

No unnecessary brass optimism.

Shoulder first.

Load.

Stable.

Forward lift.

Fine.

Side.

Better.

Overhead still bad.

Good enough.

Turnaround rules:

back at Marker Seven before late fog if route stopped making sense;

no crossing active washouts;

no climbing structural ruins;

no salvage removal;

if shoulder sharpened or crutch footing degraded beyond controlled pace, return;

if fog erased known return line, stop and wait rather than invent geography.

Adventure apparently improved when written like a warranty.

Sunroad to Seven went faster second time.

Markers.

Standing stones.

Three flashes.

Spring crossing.

Seven.

No need to rediscover things I already knew.

At Three Rays pad, fern still covered half-disk location.

Good.

Ridge recovery hadn't happened yet.

I did not touch it.

Then upper road.

Past maintained limit, Sunroad changed immediately.

Not into wilderness.

Into neglect.

Different.

Old paving still existed under grass.

Drainage channels clogged.

Iron edge posts leaned.

Tree roots lifted fitted stones into low ridges.

Wheel tracks appeared and vanished.

Somebody used this road occasionally.

Not carts recently.

Foot traffic maybe.

Goats certainly.

Fifty paces past pad stood the iron frame I'd seen yesterday.

It was taller than me seated, shorter than me standing. Two uprights, one crossbar, heavily rusted.

Three Rays mark stamped into one lower bracket.

Nothing mounted inside.

On inner faces were vertical rows of small holes.

Adjustable mounts.

Maybe.

Could have held glass strips at different heights.

Could have held signs.

Could have held bells.

I drew it.

No conclusions.

Beyond frame, road bent around ridge and lost Bracken Vale entirely.

There.

Isolation.

Not as complete as Mirror route.

Road still wide.

But no maintained marker behind line of sight.

I felt it anyway.

Choice becoming place.

The ridge rose gradually.

Fog below, clear above.

Sun bright enough to make wet leaves irritating.

At what might once have been Marker Eight, only a foundation remained.

Square socket.

Broken number stone nearby, face down.

I turned it with crutch tip enough to see one carved loop.

Could be eight.

Could be decorative.

Left it.

Farther on I found another polished standing slab.

Not one of three below.

Smaller.

Face angled west.

Base slot empty.

When I moved across road, it flashed once.

Home mark?

Maybe.

Instruction fragment:

third return

face west

light

home mark

I stood east of slab and looked west.

Sun behind my right shoulder.

Polished face flashed toward Bracken direction if I moved a few paces.

Could be return confirmation.

Could be road art.

Could be artifact support marker once paired with missing frame.

No reason to marry theory yet.

I kept going.

One bell beyond Seven, road split around a low rock spine.

Left branch lower, shaded, damp.

Right branch higher, exposed.

No map beyond Seven.

No marker.

Both looked used.

This was first real route ambiguity.

Evidence.

Old drainage channel followed lower branch.

Iron post remnants followed upper.

Which belonged to road?

Maybe both.

I backed fifty paces.

Last certain Three Rays feature: west-facing slab.

From there, line of road aimed toward upper branch.

Lower branch looked like later water/service path.

Upper more likely main road.

I chose upper.

Recorded.

Twenty minutes later, I regretted confidence.

Not because wrong.

Because outer edge started failing.

Road narrowed against steep slope.

Old retaining wall below had bulged outward.

Several paving stones tilted toward drop.

No barrier.

No recent tread.

I stopped before it.

Could cross inside edge.

Maybe.

Distance fifteen paces.

If wall failed, inside edge might go too.

No need.

Turn around?

Reasonable.

But lower branch could bypass.

Backtrack.

Try lower.

I did.

That was exploration, apparently.

Mostly returning to places you had already walked because first guess developed opinions.

Lower branch descended into trees.

Damp channel beside it carried thin water.

Path narrower but ground more stable.

After perhaps quarter bell, I heard metal.

Not bell.

A repeated light knock.

Wind-driven.

Clink.

Pause.

Clink.

I slowed.

No person voices.

No machinery rhythm.

Ahead, branch passed beneath old stone arch built into hillside.

Not bridge.

Service underpass maybe.

From arch hung a narrow cloudy glass strip in a rusted bracket.

Real.

Still mounted.

Three Rays stamp at top.

I stopped well back.

That was road structure.

Do not remove.

Glass moved slightly in wind and tapped bracket.

Cloudy strip maybe same family as Lakeward half-wheel's glass.

This one remained in situ because awkward, valueless without context, or simply overlooked?

Maybe because road office considered structure and collectors obeyed enough.

Maybe hard to remove without breaking.

Maybe fake.

Inspect visually.

The glass had three darker bands inside it.

Not painted surface.

Suspended or layered.

When sunlight crossed through leaves, one band brightened before others.

Then faded.

Interesting.

Could be directional light response.

Could be weathered glass thickness.

I moved side to side.

At one position, all three bands lit faintly.

At another, only lower.

No magic sensation obvious.

Could use line quietener? Irrelevant.

Current reader? Maybe not.

HT-LP-4? No.

Eyes sufficient.

I recorded.

Then noticed stone arch had two grooves in floor aligned with bracket.

Wheel grooves?

Guide lines?

If cart stopped here, glass could be viewed from fixed position.

Maybe route-check station.

This was stronger.

Not full system.

But physical design.

I got closer without touching bracket.

Three bands.

One cracked corner.

Bracket adjustable holes like iron frame earlier.

Sunlight intermittent through canopy made readings inconsistent.

Original road likely had fewer trees.

Again.

Current landscape corrupting old system.

Good.

Then clink stopped.

Wind died.

Fog rose.

Fast.

Not from sky.

From lower valley.

Gray pushed between trees and across branch in sheets.

Morning ridge fog returning early?

Could happen.

No rain watch did not mean no fog.

Turnaround rule.

If fog erased known return line, stop and wait rather than invent geography.

I could still see arch.

Behind me, maybe forty paces.

Beyond that branch curved.

Need return now before visibility worsened.

I turned.

Fog thickened faster than I liked.

Road underfoot still visible ten paces.

Then six.

Then four.

No panic.

Old me knew fog.

Rule one: don't speed because scared.

Rule two: maintain contact with known feature line.

Channel on left descending.

Keep channel.

Crutch tips on stone, not soft edge.

Move.

At branch junction, visibility maybe three paces.

Problem.

Upper and lower split both disappeared into gray.

I knew I had come from lower.

Which direction from junction led west?

I had turned around once already. Orientation memory good, but fog makes confidence expensive.

West-facing slab farther back if correct branch.

Instruction fragment floated up.

face west

light

home mark

No light now.

Useless.

Maybe not.

Three Rays system likely designed for fog conditions.

But missing hardware.

Do not invent function because convenient.

I checked ground.

My own recent crutch marks?

Damp soil.

Yes.

Two paired punctures and right-foot print on lower branch approach.

Fresh.

Mine.

Follow.

Good.

No ancient magic required.

I went west.

Fog got worse.

At west-facing polished slab, I nearly walked past because flash gone.

Stone itself emerged at two paces.

I stopped.

This was known feature.

Beyond it, route to Seven should be straightforward.

Except wind shifted.

Sound.

Something heavy scraped uphill.

Not animal.

Stone on stone.

Then one sharp crack.

I froze.

Retaining failure?

Could be upper branch wall.

Could be tree.

Could be nothing near me.

Sound in fog lies too.

Then road under right crutch moved.

Small.

A paving stone tilted.

Not enough to fall.

Enough.

I shifted weight left immediately.

The outer half of road ahead dropped.

No dramatic cliff collapse.

A section maybe six paces long slumped downslope with dirt and stones, disappearing into fog.

I was one pace behind failure line.

Good.

Then my left crutch slipped on loose gravel kicked by movement.

Body started rotating downhill.

This was different from rock fragment.

No time for magic maybe.

Old brain took over.

Drop right knee? No left lower leg absent.

Use right foot pivot.

Throw left crutch uphill.

Get chest toward slope.

I did.

Shoulder screamed when right crutch loaded hard.

But held.

I went down on right knee and left hand against inner dirt bank.

Not elegant.

Stopped.

Outer road continued rattling away.

Fog swallowed everything two paces beyond.

I stayed low.

Counted breaths.

No second movement immediate.

Right shoulder sharp now.

Not pop.

Not weakness.

Pain.

Crutches intact.

Right knee bruised maybe.

No open skin.

Pack still secure.

No magic.

Good.

I backed uphill along inner bank crawling more than walking until solid paving widened.

Then sat.

Action pressure satisfied, apparently.

Very considerate road.

Problem now:

My direct return route had partially failed behind/around me? Wait, failure occurred westward of slab? I was returning west from slab and road ahead failed, meaning between me and Seven. Need alternate.

Could possibly cross remaining inner edge after settle.

No.

Fresh slump in fog.

Turnaround rule: no active washouts.

So I was cut off from known route.

Upper branch?

Maybe reconnects west of failure.

Could backtrack to junction and take upper branch around unstable section.

Earlier I rejected upper because bulging retaining wall farther east. But that hazard was east of junction? Let's map.

At junction from west, upper chosen first, encountered failing wall 20 min later east. Backtracked to junction, lower branch went east and found arch. Now returning west on lower branch, at west-facing slab before junction? Hmm slab was before split west side. If at slab, west of junction already. Failure west of slab would cut road between slab and Seven. Upper/lower branches wouldn't help. Need think.

Maybe stone slab was on main road before split; yes. So failure on main road west of slab. Alternate could be old service path below/above? Need discover.

I checked immediate hillside.

Inner slope steep but rooted.

No obvious path.

Outer downslope gone.

Fog.

Could wait for visibility and inspect extent.

Best.

I waited.

Ten minutes.

Fifteen.

Fog thinned slightly.

Enough to see slump.

Road section six or seven paces gone across outer two-thirds, but inner strip maybe one foot wide remained against hillside.

Too narrow for crutches safely.

Above failure, I could see old maintenance steps cut into slope.

Three stone treads.

Then fern.

Maybe access.

Not on map.

Could climb and bypass.

Shoulder now worse from catch.

But uphill root/step route may be safer than one-foot ledge.

Inspect.

I moved to base of steps.

Old iron handloop embedded in stone.

Rusty.

Do not trust load fully.

Steps continued diagonally west above slump.

This looked intentionally built.

Maybe maintenance bypass for retaining wall.

I could use roots/crutches/sit as needed.

Turnaround became forward-through-bypass because return road blocked.

Adventure had paperwork and now appeals.

I started.

Slow.

Shoulder limited pulling.

So I moved pack separately twice.

Sat on steps.

Used right foot and left hand more.

Crutches lifted one at time.

No need for heroics.

Halfway, fog thinned enough to show drop below.

Road failure looked worse from above.

Fresh earth.

Maybe caused by saturated retaining fill plus returning fog moisture? Fog itself not enough. Earlier wall neglect. Could fail anytime.

No causal claim.

At top of bypass, steps met a narrow bench with another Three Rays iron frame.

This one partially intact.

Inside it, no glass.

But one brass toothed arc remained bolted to lower mount.

Real hardware.

Road structure.

Do not touch.

Arc looked disturbingly similar to Lakeward half-wheel.

Not exact.

This had two visible slot positions, not three? Hard to tell through corrosion.

Still.

I drew it quickly.

Then noticed bench continued west and descended back to road beyond slump.

Maintenance bypass confirmed by geometry.

Thank you dead engineers.

I followed.

Back on Sunroad.

Known side of failure.

Marker Seven somewhere west.

Fog continued thinning.

Shoulder hurt.

Now done.

No more curiosity today.

At Seven, I sat against marker and laughed once.

Not because funny.

Because road had given me exactly one extra answer and charged an unreasonable handling fee.

I checked body.

Right shoulder pain increased from morning.

Still functional.

No deformity.

Right knee scraped through trouser but skin not open? Let's say superficial abrasion maybe minor. Better concrete consequence.

Right knee had shallow scrape, no active bleeding after wipe.

Crutch shafts intact.

Tips intact.

No magic.

Protocol still 139.

I rested long enough that fog lifted below Five.

Then returned maintained road carefully.

Three standing stones flashed once sunlight returned.

Third.

Second.

First.

Home.

Not mystical.

Comforting anyway.

At Bracken Vale ridge office, clerk looked at my shoulder before my face.

"You went past Seven."

"Your observational methods are invasive."

"What happened?"

I gave route hazard first.

Fresh slump on main upper Sunroad west of west-facing slab? Need be precise enough. Let's define office sketch location as between Marker Seven station pad and first west-facing slab? But prose had slab before split and then failure west of slab. So location can be `main road west of west-facing slab, east of Marker Seven`. However if slab east of Seven? Yes upper road after Seven: frame, unknown marker8, west-facing slab, split. So returning west, slab then failure between slab and Seven. Fine.

"Fresh road-edge slump between Marker Seven and first west-facing polished slab. Roughly six to seven paces affected. Outer two-thirds gone. Inner strip too narrow for safe crutch travel."

He stopped writing.

"When?"

"Today. I was there when it moved."

He looked up.

"You hurt?"

"Bruised shoulder worse. Scraped knee. Functional."

"Anyone else?"

"Didn't see anyone."

He wrote faster.

"How'd you get around?"

"Old maintenance steps uphill. They bypass failure and rejoin west. There's a Three Rays frame on the upper bench with a brass toothed arc still bolted in place."

He looked up again.

This time artifact mattered less than road closure.

Good sign.

"You touched it?"

"No."

"Good."

"Also found mounted cloudy glass strip in old stone arch on lower branch east of split. Three Rays stamp. Still in bracket."

"That's still there?"

"Apparently."

He rubbed forehead.

"We have old note about a shade glass on service line. Thought it was gone."

Shade glass.

New term.

"Function?"

"Don't know. Note says `shade glass`, that's all I remember."

"Can I see note?"

"Tomorrow. I'm closing Sunroad west access first."

Right.

My adventure had become public works.

He went outside and flipped notice board card.

**OLD SUNROAD WEST WALK ACCESS CLOSED PENDING EDGE INSPECTION.**

That was consequence.

Not just personal excitement.

I felt slightly guilty.

Not because collapse was my fault.

Because now no one else could walk road until inspection.

Correct outcome.

Still.

The clerk said, "You saved somebody finding it by cart mentality."

"No carts past Seven."

"People ignore boards."

Fair.

"Finder payment for road failure?"

He stared at me.

"I'm joking."

"Good."

Pause.

"Maybe hazard note stipend if inspector confirms. One copper."

"I was still joking."

"Government doesn't care."

He had earned third dry line.

Bracken Vale corrupt beyond repair.

I went back bunkhouse.

Cash still fifteen.

No reward yet.

No purchase.

No artifact pocketed.

But evidence had changed again.

Three Rays hardware was not only collector objects.

On deeper Sunroad:

an adjustable iron frame;

a mounted cloudy strip with internal bands;

polished directional stone;

an old maintenance bypass;

another frame with toothed brass arc still bolted in place.

This system had been distributed through road itself.

Not one magic box.

Infrastructure.

That mattered.

Maybe Three Rays was less like artifact and more like language roads spoke.

No.

Too poetic.

Maybe it was a layered route-confirmation system using light, mounted readers, paired disks, and station hardware.

Still too much.

Need archive.

The damaged plate transcript suddenly mattered more than another deeper walk.

And Sunroad was now closed anyway.

Conveniently removing temptation.

My shoulder wanted rest.

Tomorrow:

ridge note if available;

then likely Lakeward.

Archive transcript.

Maybe re-check 9c frame if still there.

If sold, sold.

No destiny reservation.

I lay down.

The shoulder throbbed.

Knee stung.

No magic had saved me.

Old experience had.

Again.

Not power.

Positioning.

Falling correctly.

Recognizing when narrow road stopped being road.

I had wanted more adventure.

Apparently adventure had heard me.

# CHAPTER 469

## THE ARCHITECT

My shoulder hurt less in the morning.

My knee looked worse.

That was mostly because scrapes enjoyed color.

Red yesterday.

Dark red today.

A little purple around one edge because apparently my body had discovered decoration.

I sat on the Bracken bunkhouse floor with my back against the wall and did the same increasingly familiar inventory.

Head fine.

Neck fine.

Right shoulder sore, better than last night, still poor overhead.

Right knee scraped, flexion fine.

Residual limb fine.

Hands fine.

Crutches intact.

Magic still fifty-nine minimal, fifty-three shaping, one hundred thirty-nine external.

Cash fifteen copper.

Vale twenty-three.

Trial limb dry and healthy in the cage.

Lot Sixty-Seven still mine.

Sunroad closed because part of it tried to become hillside.

And for once, instead of asking what happened next, I asked a different question.

What the hell was I doing?

Not existentially.

Too early for that.

Operationally.

I had been letting the road answer for me.

A job appeared.

I took it.

A market had something strange.

I looked.

A route opened.

I followed it.

Somebody needed help.

I helped.

Somebody had a story.

I walked into the story.

It had worked surprisingly well.

I had crossed half a region that way.

Duskport.

North Reach.

Merehook.

Lerrin.

Old Merrow.

Ternhaven.

Dorrin.

Black Reed.

Brineward.

Grey Post.

High Shelf.

Mere Ridge.

Larken Lake.

Lakeward.

Morrow.

White Reed.

Sleeping Stone.

Three Sisters.

Mirror Spring.

Back through Lakeward.

Bracken Vale.

Sunroad.

I had stopped counting the trip in days somewhere along the way.

That bothered me.

Not because I needed a birthday calendar for every bunk.

Because losing count meant I had stopped measuring the journey as a project.

It had become weather.

Something happening around me.

I pulled notebook closer.

Receipts.

Lodging marks.

Cart holds.

Ferry dates where I had them.

Work notes.

Magic attempt dates.

Trial-limb wear logs.

Home letters.

Enough to reconstruct later.

Not perfectly now.

Weeks.

Definitely weeks.

Long enough that I had learned the smell of cities I had not known existed.

Long enough that home no longer shared the same air.

Long enough that I had almost died more than once.

Long enough that twenty-four copper had briefly felt rich.

That part annoyed me.

I stared at purse.

Fifteen.

Five above the floor I had invented because being reduced to nine copper while traveling sounded stupid.

It was a good floor.

It was also poverty thinking dressed as policy.

I knew the difference.

Old me had managed supply lines that could keep people alive through disasters.

Current me could buy a broken brass frame or travel four copper without being able to comfortably do both.

That was not tragic.

It was simply unacceptable as a permanent state.

I wrote a heading.

**AUDIT.**

Then six lines beneath it.

**Body.**

**Magic.**

**Money.**

**Tools.**

**Information.**

**People.**

I looked at them.

"There you are."

The man on the bunk across from me rolled over.

"What?"

"Not you."

"Good."

He went back to sleep.

I started with body because body collected interest whether I planned for it or not.

### BODY

Left below-knee amputation.

Knee good.

Right leg doing too much.

Shoulders doing too much because crutches rented both hands and charged by the hour.

Hands better than first months.

Endurance better.

Terrain judgment much better.

Climbing still expensive.

Carrying still stupid without support.

Trial work limb had already changed the shape of some tasks even before final fitting.

Both hands free.

Short carries.

Standing without making every object somebody else's problem.

The thing wasn't a leg back.

It was access to hands.

More hands.

Still true.

The finished limb cost one silver and eighteen copper total.

Twenty copper deposit already paid.

Remaining balance still ugly because silver and copper weren't one neat arithmetic line I could flatten for emotional convenience.

Fine.

Priority remained.

But there was another body problem now.

I had treated conditioning as consequence.

Walking because road required it.

Climbing because route required it.

Resting because body forced me.

That wasn't training.

Training had intent.

I knew that.

Old me had known exactly which movement failed first under fatigue.

He had known where somebody else needed to stand because he had spent years learning what his own body could cover.

Current me was still discovering limits by accidentally arriving at them.

Not enough.

I wrote:

**Recover shoulder. Resume trial wear when stable. Finish work-limb funding. Build conditioning deliberately, not only through travel. Track carry / standing / climb / recovery.**

No heroic montage.

No pretending one limb would turn me into old body.

Just less accidental weakness.

### MAGIC

Fifty-nine minimal.

Fifty-three shaping.

One hundred thirty-nine external.

That number looked impressive until you remembered what one hundred thirty-nine contained.

Trembles.

Maybe-movements.

Tiny shifts.

Ambiguous results.

A few clean nudges.

One rock that moved enough to hit shoulder instead of skull.

Useful.

Not reliable.

Old me had been support.

Not `standing in back making people glow` support.

Real support.

I had made strong people harder to kill.

Faster to understand.

Better positioned.

Less likely to waste their best attack on wrong problem.

I had controlled lines.

Created time.

Interrupted bad timing.

Protected exits.

Moved information before panic could outrun it.

Made specialists better because I was there.

At the highest level, support wasn't helping.

It was changing what everyone else in the room was capable of getting away with.

That was what I missed.

Not rank letter.

Not applause.

That feeling.

Seeing six things going wrong and making five of them stop mattering.

Current magic couldn't do that.

Current body couldn't do that.

Current purse definitely couldn't do that.

But the brain that had learned how still existed.

Mostly.

I wrote:

**Next magic goal is not bigger. Reliable support use. Ask Hessa how to turn bounded external nudge/deflection into controlled repeatability without expanding claim. Prioritize interruption / stabilization / signaling / positioning before raw force.**

That felt right.

I didn't need to move a wagon.

If I could reliably move the right small thing at the right moment, that was already support.

A latch.

A line.

A falling fragment.

A tool just out of reach.

A projectile a handspan wrong.

A signal.

A balance point.

Not claims.

Targets.

Important difference.

### MONEY

Fifteen copper.

Twenty-three with Vale.

Twenty copper already sunk as work-limb deposit.

Several artifacts owned.

Some with resale value probably.

Not liquid.

Not counting them as grocery money.

I had earned more on one real field contract than on several days of tiny road jobs combined.

There.

That was data.

The little jobs were useful when stranded.

They were not progression.

Not anymore.

Two copper for washing something was fine if I needed two copper.

It was not a career plan.

The Mirror Spring contract paid twelve because uncertainty, route risk, evidence discipline, and trust had value.

What else could I sell at that level?

Field observation.

Artifact screening maybe, once reputation justified it.

Route assessment.

Hazard documentation.

Theatre work at higher responsibility if I returned long enough to build it.

Specialized carrying/inspection less likely until leg finished.

Support work eventually.

Not yet.

Information brokerage?

Dangerous word.

But knowing where things were and who cared absolutely had value.

I wrote:

**Ten copper remains survival reserve, not goal.**

Then:

**First liquid target: 30c cash above ordinary immediate needs, not counting Vale, limb deposit, or owned gear.**

Thirty wasn't wealth.

It was enough that a nine-copper artifact could appear and I wouldn't have to choose between opportunity and being one bad bunk away from stupidity.

I underlined it.

**Stop celebrating survival-floor liquidity as success. Build opportunity money.**

There.

That one felt mean.

Good.

### TOOLS

I opened the cage and pulled pack out.

Inventory.

Retired line quietener.

Useful for false/echo tone on short copper, with real risk of suppressing weak real signal.

WS-F/9 current reader.

Directional aid.

Context-heavy.

HT-LP-4 load tile.

Last sustained load memory.

Useful.

Lot Sixty-Seven.

Comparative timing, now actually supported under controlled delayed reference.

Still no seconds-per-mark.

Road-test limb.

Not mine.

Not finished.

Tavi's striped rock.

Not a tool.

Stayed anyway.

Notebook.

Probably most valuable item by hours invested.

What was missing?

Communication.

Reliable signaling.

Position confirmation.

Something that gave me hands without costing all mobility.

Something that helped other people, not just me.

Three Rays suddenly looked different.

Not collector interesting.

Support interesting.

A distributed road system that helped travelers confirm position, direction, timing, home line through fog?

If that was even half true, it was exactly the kind of technology old me would have loved.

Not because it made one person stronger.

Because it made everybody using route less likely to become lost.

That was support infrastructure.

I wrote:

**Three Rays: investigate as support system, not treasure. What problem did distributed frames/disks/shade glass solve for ordinary travelers?**

Then:

**Do not buy component until function / pairing / compatibility evidence crosses threshold.**

Still disciplined.

Unfortunately.

### INFORMATION

This one was embarrassing.

I had learned a lot.

I had not organized access.

Carrow knew things.

Merehook knew things.

Dorrin knew things.

Lakeward knew things.

Bracken knew things.

I had notes.

But notes were not a network unless I could ask them questions later.

I wrote names and places carefully.

Hessa.

Magic evidence and safety.

Sori Vale.

Commercial judgment, custody, money eyes, questions I didn't always enjoy.

Hark & Venn.

Limb work and body/tool reality.

Mabel Crowe.

White Reed, local route memory, water behavior, no patience.

Helena Sol.

Morrow cooperative, practical records, willingness to say unpleasant thing out loud.

Jonah Sol.

Young, smart enough to challenge me, route/family link.

Bracken ridge office.

Not a relationship yet.

An institution I now knew how to access.

Lakeward district archive.

Same.

Theatre.

Home workplace.

People there knew me in a way road clerks didn't.

Cala.

Tavi.

Lyssa.

Home was not one person.

That mattered.

I stopped.

Support.

There it was again.

Old me had never been powerful alone.

That was one of those lies people told after the fact because it made stories easier.

God-tier support without people was a very expensive man standing in a field.

My strongest abilities had only mattered because somebody else was doing something worth supporting.

Current trip had made my network wider.

But thin.

A lot of people would remember `that one-legged guy who asked too many questions.`

Fewer would answer a letter.

Fewer would change plans because I asked.

That was okay.

Networks had levels too.

I made columns.

**Home.**

**Trusted expertise.**

**Work.**

**Commercial.**

**Route / regional.**

**Maybe.**

I did not put people into neat boxes permanently.

Just enough to see gaps.

No healer I trusted nearby.

No regular combat partner.

No one traveling with me who could cover mobility failures.

No artifact specialist I trusted yet.

No reliable courier network beyond public post.

No serious capital source that didn't come with obligations I might hate.

No person out here who would notice quickly if I vanished.

That last one sat differently.

I had home.

I had people.

I was also far enough away that disappearance had delay built into it.

Support network wasn't only who cared.

It was who could act in time.

I underlined:

**Maintain people while away. Send useful letters, not only status notes. Ask questions. Return favors. Build reasons people answer.**

That was uncomfortable because it sounded social.

I had survived worse.

### PEOPLE

Already half done.

But this category wasn't names.

It was role.

Who was around me right now?

Nobody.

A bunkhouse full of strangers.

A ridge clerk who knew me as the guy who reported a road collapse.

A district office that might eventually pay one copper for hazard notes.

That was it.

I had chosen solo travel.

I still liked solo travel.

But support specialist traveling indefinitely with no support network was funny in a way I disliked.

I did not suddenly want a party.

Absolutely not.

Three people arguing over camp duties sounded worse than fog.

But I needed to stop treating recurring relationships as accidental souvenirs.

I wrote:

**No party requirement. Build constellation, not entourage.**

Then:

**People should know what I'm good for. I should know what they're good for. Reciprocity, not collecting friends.**

That sounded like old me.

More than anything else on page.

I stared at six headings.

Body.

Magic.

Money.

Tools.

Information.

People.

Six support layers.

Current status?

Body: functional, limited, improving, unfinished.

Magic: evidence of potential, unreliable.

Money: not starving, still broke enough that interesting opportunities hurt.

Tools: weird and growing, not coherent.

Information: broad, badly indexed socially.

People: real relationships, mostly behind me.

That was not failure.

It was baseline.

There was a difference.

I had spent too long treating current limits as verdicts.

Then the road had started changing that.

The trial limb had changed it.

Magic attempt one hundred thirty-eight had changed it.

The rock deflection had changed it.

Mirror Spring had changed it.

Sunroad had changed it.

Not because any of them made me powerful.

Because they reminded me I was allowed to build.

Old me had once climbed from weak to S-class.

Not this body.

Not this world exactly.

Not these tools.

But I knew what deliberate improvement felt like.

It did not feel inspirational.

It felt administrative.

Good.

I could do administrative.

I packed the notebook.

Sunroad was closed.

Current job was therefore information.

Lakeward archive.

Finish Three Rays enough to decide whether the nine-copper half-wheel was support equipment, collector bait, or both.

Then stop drifting.

I checked route board.

Return cart to Lakeward: four copper.

Of course.

Fifteen to eleven.

One above survival reserve.

I hated that immediately.

Good.

Useful emotion.

I paid four.

Eleven.

No tiny job first.

No washing crates to make number prettier.

I was spending deliberately on information that might alter future acquisition decisions.

That was different.

Still expensive.

On cart, I added one more line under MONEY.

**Travel that increases leverage can be investment. Travel because bored is expense. Know difference.**

Bracken Vale disappeared behind ridge.

Lakeward returned by afternoon.

I went directly to district archive before market.

That was deliberate too.

Archive access to the Sunroad repair packet cost one copper for reading desk and copied extracts.

Eleven to ten.

Exactly floor.

I felt poorer than I had yesterday despite knowing more.

There was lesson.

The archive clerk brought a thin packet.

No name needed.

Most of it was bridge-repair accounting.

Stone.

Labor.

Timber.

Drainage.

Then recovery inventory I had already seen.

Then partial transcript of copper instruction plate.

More survived than Bracken rubbing.

Not much more.

I copied exact fragments:

**... THIRD RETURN ...**

**... FRAME FACE WEST ...**

**... PASS LIGHT THROUGH SHADE ...**

**... HOME MARK ...**

**... MATCH PAIR BEFORE ...**

Then corrosion gap.

Then:

**... DO NOT READ SINGLE DISC ...**

I stopped.

There.

That mattered.

Not full manual.

Enough.

Single disk explicitly not readable alone, at least under whatever instruction survived.

Echo Step orphan disk at eighteen copper became less attractive immediately.

Paired disks mattered.

Frame faced west.

Shade glass transmitted light.

Home mark.

Third return.

System.

Still unclear.

But system.

Another sheet described catalog notes from recovered components.

Three brass frames were similar but not identical.

Six disks carried paired stamped numbers.

Cloudy glass strips each had three density bands.

One frame showed wear on an internal tooth range corresponding to only part of its arc.

No preserved claim of magic.

One archivist note suggested devices might have compared **sun position against installed road markers**.

Another, later note argued they may have been **fog-route confirmation devices using paired station settings**.

Neither proven.

Excellent.

Competing interpretations.

I copied both.

Then asked whether any surviving complete system could be inspected publicly.

"Roadworks collection has one reconstructed set," clerk said.

"Working?"

"Demonstration working, according to their notice. Not certified original behavior. Some replacement parts."

"Where?"

"Lakeward east museum storehouse. Open two afternoons a week. Next is tomorrow."

There.

Useful.

Not destiny.

Schedule.

"Fee?"

"Three bits general floor. Demonstration depends staff."

Manageable.

I left archive with ten copper exactly.

Then went to market.

The half-wheel stall had changed.

The space where brass frame sat yesterday held two cracked lantern housings and a box of spoon handles.

I knew before asking.

Still asked.

"Road frame?"

Seller looked up.

"Sold this morning."

There it was.

"Nine?"

"Eight and six bits. Buyer took two other brass scraps too."

I nodded.

Not mine.

No tragedy.

But I felt it.

Not because I knew frame was valuable.

Because I had been one information step too slow and too cash-thin to act when opportunity existed.

That was exactly the problem.

The seller said, "You should've bought it yesterday."

"I didn't know enough yesterday."

"Now you do?"

"More."

"Same thing to collectors."

"That's why collectors get robbed."

He laughed.

I didn't.

Nine copper would have broken my floor yesterday.

Today I had ten total.

Correct decision remained correct.

Still hurt.

Support meant options.

Money was support.

There it was in simplest possible form.

If I wanted to be the man who could act on rare information, I needed enough reserve that action didn't threaten food and travel.

Thirty copper target stopped feeling arbitrary.

It felt small.

I walked away from market without buying anything.

At cheap food stall I spent bits.

Then found public post desk.

I wrote three letters.

Not status broadcasts.

Requests.

First to Hessa.

**External work has now reached 139 attempts. #138 was a bounded clean small shift around one pace under controlled conditions. #139 was emergency deflection of moving stone fragment during a slide; visible lateral change was enough to turn head/face impact into shoulder clip, but geometry/adrenaline/moving-target effects are unresolved and I did not repeat. When I return, I want to discuss a deliberate support-use goal: reliable small interruption/deflection or stabilization before chasing range or force. I do not want to generalize #139. I want to know what evidence sequence would actually justify training toward repeatability. - Greg**

Good.

Second to Hark & Venn.

**Road trial build remains healthy. I have deliberately rested it through rough-route travel and a bruised shoulder. No new skin issue. Last stable wear remains 25 minutes at Mere Ridge with short two-hand carry. I am still traveling. I want the finished work limb badly enough that I'm changing money priorities around it. When practical, please send current remaining requirements beyond balance: fitting checkpoints, expected return visits, any material lead time, and whether extended travel changes what you need from my wear log. - Greg**

Third was harder.

To Sori.

**I have a money question, unfortunately. The trip is making it obvious that tiny jobs preserve liquidity but do not build it. A recent field contract paid 12c because route risk, evidence handling, and bounded reporting were actually valuable. I'm trying to identify higher-value work I can credibly sell without pretending to expertise I don't have. If you see categories where my field notes / artifact screening / route assessment / commercial judgment are worth more than day labor, tell me what would make someone pay for them and what proof/reputation I'd need. Also: if I start calling this `capital planning`, you're allowed to make fun of me once. - Greg**

That one felt like asking for help.

Good.

Support network required asking.

Postage bits.

No copper count change.

I considered letters to home.

Then wrote one combined note for Cala/Tavi/Lyssa and theatre route, less formal.

**Still east. Finished Morrow field job, then followed an old roadworks/artifact trail to Bracken Vale. Sunroad partly collapsed while I was on it; I'm fine beyond bruised shoulder and scraped knee, and access is now closed pending inspection. Back in Lakeward. I realized I've been letting the trip set my priorities for me, so I'm changing that. I want to finish the work leg, rebuild magic deliberately, stop being this broke, and get better at the support work I used to be good at. Not coming home immediately, but I am thinking about home as part of the plan instead of the place I eventually wander back to. - Greg**

I read last sentence twice.

Kept it.

Then I opened notebook to AUDIT page again.

Under each heading, I wrote one next action.

**Body: shoulder recovery, then trial wear.**

**Magic: Hessa reply / repeatability plan.**

**Money: 30c liquid target, seek higher-value work.**

**Tools: Three Rays demonstration tomorrow, no orphan purchases.**

**Information: archive + route contacts, organize correspondence.**

**People: maintain constellation, ask instead of only report.**

Six lines.

Nothing magical happened.

I remained one-legged.

Bronze.

Ten copper.

Shoulder sore.

No Three Rays frame.

No sudden revelation that I had secretly been strong all along.

Good.

I knew exactly how weak I was.

That was useful information too.

The difference was that weak had stopped being description of what happened to me.

It was now starting point of something I intended to change.

I had done this once before.

Not same way.

Not same world.

Not same body.

But I knew the first real step.

You stop asking whether progress will happen.

You decide what you're building.

Then you make tomorrow answer to it.

# CHAPTER 470

## THE CALIBRATOR

Ten copper looked different after I wrote thirty at the top of a page.

Before, ten had been safety.

Now it looked like the bottom of a hole.

Not a deep hole.

Still a hole.

I counted anyway.

Ten copper.

Twenty-three with Vale.

Bits in the small pouch, enough that I did not need to turn breakfast into a philosophical crisis.

Work-limb deposit already paid.

Everything else was either equipment, obligation, or wishful thinking.

I closed the purse.

Lakeward morning came through the bunkhouse shutters in thin gray bars.

My shoulder had improved again overnight, though improvement had apparently decided to become a slow administrative process.

Forward lift was fine.

Side lift pulled.

Overhead still produced a sharp enough warning that I stopped before proving anything.

Right knee scrape had dried clean.

No swelling.

No reason to dramatize it.

The reconstructed Three Rays demonstration opened after midday.

That gave me half a day.

Old Greg, meaning me three weeks ago or possibly three months ago because my timekeeping had become criminal, would have wandered the market until something happened.

Current Greg had a page.

**BODY**

**MAGIC**

**MONEY**

**TOOLS**

**INFORMATION**

**PEOPLE**

I hated how much it looked like work.

Good plans often did.

Body first.

Not training.

Assessment.

The trial limb had been rested through the Sunroad push and shoulder fall. Skin had been healthy last check. Lakeward had dry paved courtyards and benches.

But using it while shoulder was sore could actually reduce crutch load if I kept the session short and flat.

Or it could make me stupidly confident because both hands became available and I forgot I was still testing an unfinished shop leg.

I took it to the bunkhouse rear court.

No audience except a woman hanging sheets who looked once, decided I was not interesting enough, and continued working.

Perfect.

Socket.

Liner.

Temporary ash shaft.

Medium test foot.

Everything looked intact.

I fitted slowly.

Standing changed the room.

Still.

Every time.

Crutches under hands, weight mostly right, then a little through left.

No immediate pressure complaint.

I waited.

Then one step.

Then another.

Dry flat stone.

The body map came back faster than before.

Not natural.

Familiar.

Different thing.

I walked the length of the courtyard once with both crutches.

Turned.

Came back.

Shoulder tolerated it.

Then I put one crutch against wall and stood with the other in right hand.

Left hand free.

That was the whole damned point.

Not walking pretty.

Not looking restored.

A hand.

One available hand changed support work immediately.

Carry a signal lamp.

Hold a line.

Open a latch.

Use an artifact.

Catch somebody.

Point without putting a crutch under armpit and hoping gravity respected optimism.

I picked up an empty wash basin from bench.

Light.

Both hands for three steps.

Set it down.

No wobble beyond normal test-leg awkwardness.

Again.

Five steps.

Stop.

No reason to turn progress into punishment.

I checked time.

Twelve minutes.

I could have done more.

That was exactly why I stopped.

Socket off.

Immediate skin check.

Mild pink at upper contact.

No point heat.

No pain.

Twenty minutes later, mostly clear.

Thirty, clear.

Notebook:

**Lakeward dry court. 12 min. Both-crutch walking stable. One-crutch standing brief. Empty basin two-hand carry 3 + 5 steps. Mild upper pink immediate, mostly clear 20, clear 30. Shoulder tolerated. Stopped early intentionally.**

Then beneath it:

**BODY TARGET: not duration. usable hands.**

That felt better.

The old support version of me had never cared how impressive a movement looked if it failed to create options.

Good.

One lane aligned.

Money next.

Harder.

I went to Lakeward district contracting board instead of general work board.

Different building.

Different wages.

Also different assumptions.

General board had washing, loading, carrying, sweeping, sorting.

Contract board had things like:

**ROUTE CONDITION COPY / NORTH REED / 6C / TWO DAYS / OWN TRAVEL**

**FERRY LOSS WITNESS / VARIABLE / LICENSED OR DISTRICT REFERENCE**

**WAREHOUSE CLAIM INVENTORY / 8C / TWO REFERENCES**

**FIELD MARKER AUDIT / 7C / ROUTE EXPERIENCE / MAP COPY REQUIRED**

**PRIVATE SALVAGE CATALOG / RATE BY LOT / PRIOR APPRAISAL REFERENCE**

There.

Money did not magically appear because I wanted more of it.

The higher-paying work had gates.

References.

Records.

Proof.

Sometimes licenses.

Annoying.

Also useful.

I copied categories and requirements.

A clerk at side desk watched me.

"Looking for work?"

"Looking for what work pays more than two copper."

He nodded like this was a common disease.

"Usually work where being wrong costs somebody more than two copper."

That was good enough that I wrote it down.

"How does somebody get onto those without already being on them?"

"Reference."

"From?"

"Someone who paid you for comparable work. District office. Registered shop. Merchant house. Road office. Depends on listing."

Mirror Spring.

Bracken hazard report.

Morrow route notes.

Maybe.

"Does completed district supplemental sample work count?"

He looked up.

"If chain accepted and hiring office signs field completion."

"It was."

"Then get a completion slip."

Simple.

I had receipt proving sample entered transport.

Not completion reference.

Different paper.

"Road hazard report?"

"If ridge office confirms useful and identifies you. Better if closure or repair action followed."

Sunroad had closed.

That mattered.

"Artifact screening?"

"Registered appraiser reference."

I had Sori.

Maybe.

Not assume.

The clerk pulled a blank card.

"You trying to build a field file?"

"Apparently."

"Then stop collecting stories about work and collect signatures."

Less poetic than grandmother route lore.

Probably more profitable.

I thanked him.

No name.

Didn't need one.

My people audit had been too emotional yesterday.

Who would answer a letter.

Who cared.

Important.

But support networks also had institutional forms.

Who could verify what I had actually done?

That was different.

I added a second people list:

**REFERENCE CAPACITY**

Morrow district field completion.

Bracken ridge hazard usefulness.

Sori commercial/artifact judgment if earned.

Hark & Venn limb/travel reliability, probably not field contracting.

Theatre work reliability maybe Carrow local.

Hessa magic work absolutely not something I was turning into employment paperwork without asking.

Good.

People were not resources.

References were specific things people might choose to provide.

Difference mattered.

I spent no copper.

Already improving.

By midday, the east museum storehouse had a line.

Not large.

Eight people.

Three looked like collectors.

One looked like a teacher with four children attached by moral force.

I paid three bits and entered.

The Three Rays reconstruction occupied a long room with shutters on both east and west walls.

Not impressive at first glance.

Three waist-high iron frames sat on a wooden track about twelve paces long.

Each frame held a cloudy glass strip.

At the center of each was a black disk mounted behind the glass.

Brass toothed arcs allowed the disk assembly to rotate through limited positions.

On the wall above them:

**RECONSTRUCTION FROM SUNROAD RECOVERY RECORDS**

**REPLACEMENT FRAMES / REPLACEMENT GLASS IN TWO STATIONS**

**ONE ORIGINAL DISK PAIR USED FOR DEMONSTRATION**

**BEHAVIOR ILLUSTRATIVE, NOT CERTIFIED TO HISTORIC FIELD PERFORMANCE**

Good museum.

A staff demonstrator waited until group settled.

Also unnamed.

"This reconstruction is intended to show one plausible operating principle supported by surviving instructions and component wear. It is not a claim that every Sunroad station functioned identically."

I liked her too.

The children did not.

One whispered, "Does it do anything?"

His teacher whispered back, "That's what she's explaining."

"But does it?"

Strong question.

The demonstrator opened west shutters.

Afternoon light entered low enough to strike first shade glass.

She rotated brass arc two teeth.

The cloudy strip darkened most of the light except one narrow band.

Behind it, black disk showed a pale crescent.

She walked to second station.

Different frame position.

Different disk.

Another pale crescent.

Then third.

At the end she held up a small white board with three drawn crescent shapes.

"The paired disks carry numbered notch families. Under this reconstruction, a traveler or road keeper could compare the observed light shape at one station against the expected paired shape at another. If the pair and frame orientation agreed, the station could provide a directional or timing confirmation even when distant landmarks were obscured."

One collector raised hand.

"So navigation."

"Possibly route confirmation. Not general navigation."

Good.

"Fog?"

"Direct heavy fog would reduce useful light. Surviving notes suggest shade or broken fog conditions may have been relevant. We do not know operating thresholds."

"Magic?"

"No charged material has been identified in recovered standard components."

Interesting.

Mechanical optical support infrastructure.

Maybe.

The demonstrator changed one thing.

She swapped the second black disk with another from tray.

Same size.

Different stamped number.

Ran light again.

The crescent did not match the board.

"This is why the surviving instruction fragment `do not read single disc` matters. A disk alone does not tell us enough. The relationship between paired markings, frame orientation, and expected light condition appears to have carried the useful information."

There.

The orphan disk at eighteen copper became even worse as a purchase.

Not useless.

But context-dependent.

A component without pair or station knowledge.

Collector bait unless you had matching infrastructure.

The child whispered, louder now:

"So it tells you if you're where you think you are?"

The demonstrator paused.

"That is one plausible simplified description."

The child nodded.

Solved.

I wrote:

**Three Rays support function candidate: externalized confirmation. Not tell traveler where to go. Help detect when route/timing/orientation doesn't match expectation. Reduces confident wrongness.**

That was support.

Deeply.

Not power.

A system designed around people making fewer expensive mistakes.

I understood why it bothered me in a good way.

Old me would have loved it.

Not because artifact.

Because it moved uncertainty out of one person's head and into shared infrastructure.

You didn't need the best navigator on every cart.

You needed stations, paired components, expected patterns, maintenance, and people trained enough to compare.

That made ordinary travelers better.

Support at civic scale.

I stayed for second demonstration.

Not because first was unclear.

Because I wanted to watch failure.

I asked staff afterward whether they demonstrated misalignment deliberately.

She did.

Frame faced slightly wrong.

Same pair.

Same light.

Crescent shifted enough to miss expected board.

Then she partially shaded window.

Signal weakened.

Not binary.

Interpretation got harder.

Good.

Constraints.

"Could somebody fake a match by rotating frame until shape looked right?" I asked.

"In reconstruction? Yes."

"Historic system?"

"If frame orientation was uncontrolled, presumably that would defeat part of purpose. Installed frames show anchor points and wear consistent with limited set positions. Whether there were seals, stops, inspection rules, or other controls is unresolved."

Another support lesson.

Systems only worked if people couldn't casually tune evidence to answer they wanted.

Morrow again.

Different object.

Same species.

I left without buying anything.

That was becoming a skill.

At the archive desk, I asked whether reconstructed-set notes could be copied.

Two bits for public summary.

I paid.

Bits, not copper.

Fine.

The summary added no miracle.

Paired-number families.

Three density bands.

Frame orientation west at some documented stations.

Return/home marks in damaged text.

Installed tooth wear suggesting repeated limited positions.

No charged material in standard recovered parts.

Possible optical route/timing confirmation.

Unresolved regional variation.

Enough.

Three Rays had moved from `maybe buy weird brass` to `understand support architecture`.

That changed tool priority.

I crossed **orphan Three Rays component** off acquisition list.

Then wrote:

**Desired tool function: independent position / condition confirmation that works without full station network.**

Different.

Maybe another artifact could do it.

Maybe current reader plus maps plus ordinary markers already covered some.

Don't buy history when you need capability.

That sentence hurt collectors everywhere.

Late afternoon, I went back to contracting board.

Not to take work.

To look again with new eyes.

Seven copper marker audit.

Two references.

Route experience.

Map copy.

I had route experience.

Could get references.

Not today.

Eight copper warehouse claim inventory.

Two references.

I had done custody/evidence work around artifacts and field samples, but not enough formal warehouse claim inventory to pretend equivalence.

Private salvage catalog.

Prior appraisal reference.

Sori maybe.

Again, maybe.

The money lane was becoming concrete.

Not `find better job`.

Build proof that unlocked better categories.

I wrote:

**MONEY PROGRESSION IS CREDENTIAL + REPUTATION + CAPITAL, NOT ONLY HOURS.**

Then underneath:

**Immediate: get Morrow completion reference. get Bracken hazard confirmation. wait for Sori reply.**

Thirty copper stopped looking impossible.

Still not close.

Different.

At post counter, no replies.

Of course.

I had sent letters yesterday.

The universe remained committed to distance.

I almost laughed at myself.

People network required patience.

Terrible mechanic.

I bought dinner with bits and returned to bunkhouse.

Shoulder tired but not worse from twelve-minute limb session.

That was useful data.

I checked skin again hours later.

Clear.

Right knee scrape boring.

Best possible condition for knee scrape.

I opened notebook to six headings.

BODY:

Twelve-minute stable session. Hands target clarified.

MAGIC:

Waiting Hessa. No test.

MONEY:

Ten copper. Thirty target. Higher-pay categories identified. Need references.

TOOLS:

Three Rays system useful conceptually. Orphan component poor acquisition. Capability gap reframed.

INFORMATION:

Demonstration confirms plausible paired optical route-check principle, reconstruction only.

PEOPLE:

Separate relationship from reference. Ask specifically. Don't assume.

That was a better day than earning two copper.

Financially, I had earned nothing.

That part still annoyed me.

But I had moved earning ceiling.

Potentially.

No applause.

No stat screen.

No satisfying blue light announcing **FIELD CONTRACTOR UNLOCKED**.

Just paperwork I didn't have yet.

Which meant tomorrow needed action.

Not combat.

Progression action.

Get the references moving.

Find out whether Lakeward had a paid task where one existing accepted field completion could qualify me, even if rate wasn't eight copper yet.

Maybe take something that built the second reference rather than merely paid lunch.

That was the shift.

A two-copper job could still be worth taking if it bought access to seven-copper work later.

A five-copper job could be bad if it taught nothing, proved nothing, and stranded me somewhere useless.

Money wasn't just amount.

It had direction.

Same with magic.

Same with tools.

Same with people.

Support was never one big thing.

It was a stack of small advantages arranged before somebody needed them.

I had forgotten that because I'd spent so long being the person who needed the advantages.

That was fair.

Reset had taken almost everything.

Body.

Power.

Money.

Status.

Infrastructure.

People who knew what I could do.

I had treated survival as proof I was rebuilding.

It was proof I was alive.

Different category.

Now I had a trial leg that could give me both hands for five careful steps.

One hundred thirty-nine external attempts, with a couple small results worth respecting and one emergency result worth not lying about.

Ten copper.

A strange little support kit.

A notebook full of roads.

People scattered across enough places that letters had become a real tool.

And, apparently, the beginning of a field file.

Pathetic compared with S-class.

Excellent compared with nothing.

I could work with excellent compared with nothing.

I turned to a clean page.

At top:

**BUILD THE STACK.**

Then, because I knew myself:

**DO NOT TURN LIFE INTO A CHECKLIST.**

Important safety note.

I closed notebook.

Tomorrow I would go make somebody willing to sign something useful.

After that, maybe the next road.

This time I intended to know what I was taking with me.

# CHAPTER 471

## THE STRATEGIST

The highest-leverage thing I could do was not train.

I hated that answer enough to trust it.

Magic was obvious.

If I could turn one hundred thirty-nine external attempts into something reliable, that changed fights, field work, rescue, positioning, probably half the support problems I cared about.

But Hessa had not answered yet.

Training without a plan would mostly produce more numbers.

Artifacts were tempting.

They could change everything.

They also cost money I did not have.

The road-test limb might change my daily operating rules more than almost anything else.

Also cost money I did not have.

People could be the biggest lever of all.

One right person could open work, training, information, equipment, transport, introductions, maybe things I had not thought to ask for yet.

Someone from my old life would be worse.

Better.

Dangerously better.

If I ran into one of the people who had known me when I was actually good, the whole progression map could change in an afternoon.

Which was exactly why I could not call that a plan.

`Find former S-class contacts through convenient coincidence` was not strategy.

It was wishing with paperwork.

So.

Money.

More specifically, earning power.

Not because coins were sacred.

Because coins converted.

Coins became the work leg.

Coins became travel.

Coins became paid archive access, better rooms when recovery mattered, specialist time, artifact opportunities, emergency food, ferry seats, training supplies, replacement crutch parts, the ability to say no to a terrible job, the ability to say yes to a rare one.

Money was support before the emergency happened.

Ten copper meant I was still one expensive decision from being broke again.

Thirty was not wealth.

Thirty was the first point where I might stop treating every useful object like a moral test.

So that was it.

Highest leverage, current conditions:

**INCREASE EARNING CEILING.**

Everything else stayed alive.

But until something changed the calculation, that was the lane.

I wrote one line under it.

**Do not confuse highest leverage with only thing that matters.**

Important.

Then I went to get proof.

Lakeward district office opened early.

The same building that had swallowed the Mirror Spring bottle now contained six counters, three queues, and a wall clock that made everyone resent time professionally.

I had the sample-transport receipt.

I had Morrow contract copy.

I had my route notes.

I did not have a field-completion reference.

The clerk at first counter looked at receipt.

"You need records."

"Which records?"

"Field services, second floor."

Second floor sent me to water contracts.

Water contracts sent me back down one floor because the supplemental sample had been private cooperative hire using district chain procedure, not district hire.

Good distinction.

Bad stairs.

My shoulder had improved enough that stairs were annoying rather than insulting.

Still.

By third counter I understood the actual problem.

Morrow had paid me.

District had accepted the bottle.

Neither fact automatically meant Lakeward would certify my whole field performance.

Correct.

I had been trying to turn `I did a thing` into `institution confirms I can do this kind of thing.`

Different claim.

A records woman read my contract more carefully than anyone so far.

Unnamed.

Blessings.

"What exactly do you want us to certify?"

Good question.

"That the sealed supplemental sample was returned under accepted chain, that my field form was complete enough for transport acceptance, and that the route report was received with it."

"Not that your route observations were correct?"

"You don't know that."

She looked up.

"Not that your judgment was good?"

"You don't know that either."

"Not that you completed every contract term satisfactorily?"

"Morrow paid me for that."

"Then Morrow can certify that part."

There.

Narrow enough.

She pulled a small form.

Not a recommendation.

A verification.

**SUPPLEMENTAL FIELD CHAIN RECEIPT**

It stated that Greg had delivered a sealed Mirror Spring source sample with intact seal and complete transport form, and that associated route/water notes were accepted into the file.

No praise.

No `excellent adventurer.`

Perfect.

"Fee?" I asked.

"No fee for a completion-copy attached to your own chain receipt."

Best price in Lakeward.

She stamped it.

First proof.

Morrow still needed separate confirmation for contract completion if I wanted the stronger reference.

I could send request.

So I did.

Short.

**To Morrow lower-channel cooperative:**

**Request written confirmation that Mirror Spring field contract was completed and paid under agreed terms, including route notes and valid sealed sample return. District has separately verified chain acceptance. Please state only what you can directly certify. - Greg**

Bits postage.

Worth it.

Then Bracken.

I went to road desk.

Sunroad closure notice had already reached Lakeward regional board.

Not full inspection result.

Just closure.

I showed my hazard-report copy.

Desk clerk checked closure notice number against it.

"Ridge office hasn't filed final inspection yet."

"I know."

"Then we can't certify hazard accuracy."

"Can you certify that my report preceded the closure?"

He checked timestamps.

"Yes."

Interesting.

Not enough for full road reference.

Still something.

He wrote a notation:

**REPORT RECEIVED PRIOR TO TEMPORARY WEST-ACCESS CLOSURE. FINAL HAZARD FINDING PENDING RIDGE INSPECTION.**

That was not a recommendation either.

Good.

Two narrow proofs.

Neither made me qualified for everything.

Together they made me look less like a man inventing his own résumé.

Contract board next.

The same clerk from yesterday recognized me.

"Signatures?"

"Partial."

I showed him.

He read both.

"Water one counts as district field-chain reference for listings that accept one. Road one doesn't count yet as road reference."

"So what does one field-chain reference unlock?"

He pulled the current cards.

Not seven-copper marker audit.

Needed route reference.

Not eight-copper warehouse claim inventory.

Needed two references.

Not ferry loss witness at upper rate.

License or two references.

One card:

**TRANSFER SEAL WITNESS / EAST LANDING / 5C / HALF DAY / ONE DISTRICT FIELD REFERENCE / NO APPRAISAL AUTHORITY**

There.

Five copper.

Half day.

More important:

If completed cleanly, landing office could verify a second kind of field work.

"What is it?"

"Inbound cargo transfer. Three crates had seal-number mismatch between ferry manifest and receiving slate yesterday. Owner wants transfer witnessed before they're moved into bonded storage. You're not valuing cargo. You're not deciding claim. You verify seals, crate marks, manifest entries, and who handles what."

Evidence custody.

Support work.

"Why five?"

"Because if somebody swaps a crate and the witness record is bad, claim gets expensive."

His sentence from yesterday, made specific.

Being wrong cost somebody more than five copper.

"How many witnesses?"

"Two. Landing office assigns one. Contract hires second outside witness."

"Why outside?"

"Owner requested independent second record."

"Any lifting?"

"No. If they ask you to lift, tell them to hire porter."

Shoulder approved.

"Could this count as second reference?"

"If landing office signs completion, yes for some custody/witness listings. Not route work. Not appraisal."

Specific.

Good.

I took it.

No grand adventure.

No monster.

No magic.

Five copper and a second rung.

That was leverage.

East Landing smelled like rope, lake water, fish scales, and old wood soaked too many times.

The three crates sat under a striped awning separated from other freight.

Medium-sized.

Iron corner bands.

Waxed cord seals through latch plates.

One landing officer stood at table with manifest slate.

Another man in merchant coat represented owner.

Neither needed names.

I gave the clerk my contract slip and district field-chain reference.

He looked at crutches.

"You're witness two?"

"Yes."

"Good. You write?"

"Often against my better judgment."

"Then write legibly."

Fair.

Rules first.

We would not open crates.

We would record:

manifest seal number;

physical seal number;

crate side mark;

existing damage;

transfer handlers;

bonded-storage bay;

any break in direct sight.

If sight broke, record it.

If seal broke, stop.

If crate mark differed, stop.

Simple.

The first crate explained why there was a job.

Manifest:

**E-7714**

Physical seal:

**E-7174**

Same digits.

Different order.

Merchant representative said, "Transcription."

Landing officer said, "Probably."

I said nothing.

Probably did not change seal.

Side mark matched owner lot.

Cord intact.

Wax intact.

No obvious tamper.

We recorded mismatch exactly.

Second crate:

Manifest **E-7715**

Seal **E-7715**.

Side mark matched.

One crushed lower corner.

Old or new?

Unknown.

Merchant said it was old.

Landing officer said damage wasn't on arrival slate.

That mattered.

"Was arrival slate written before unload or after?" I asked.

Officer checked.

"After unload."

"Then absence on slate doesn't prove damage happened after landing."

Merchant looked at me.

"Thank you."

"Also doesn't prove it was old."

He looked less thankful.

Balanced service.

We recorded:

**lower corner crushed; timing unknown; not listed on arrival slate completed after unload.**

Third crate:

Manifest **E-7716**.

Seal **E-7716**.

Side mark did not match.

Manifest owner lot mark ended **C2**.

Crate ended **G2**.

Stop.

Merchant representative swore quietly.

Landing officer called supervisor.

No crate moved.

Good system.

Supervisor checked ferry manifest copy.

Same **C2**.

Checked dock chalk board.

That showed **G2**.

So discrepancy existed between ferry paperwork and landing board, not necessarily crate swap.

Could be manifest transcription.

Could be wrong crate unloaded.

Could be owner lot mark copied wrong earlier.

Unknown.

The merchant wanted bonded storage immediately because the awning area wasn't secure enough for overnight.

Landing office would not transfer under the current manifest without correction or explicit discrepancy hold.

This was why witnesses existed.

Not to solve.

To prevent uncertainty from becoming invisible during movement.

Support.

Again.

The supervisor authorized a sealed discrepancy hold cart.

Crate could move under a separate red tag without pretending marks matched.

I watched tag attached.

Recorded tag number.

Recorded handlers.

Recorded cart.

Walked beside transfer line using crutches, not trial limb.

No need to make every progression lane move at once.

At bonded bay, crate placed separately from the two clear-transfer crates.

Seal intact.

Red tag intact.

Sight never broke.

Done.

Then the merchant representative pointed at first crate.

"If the seal number is obviously just reversed, can we correct it on witness sheet?"

"You can add corrected interpretation as a note."

"But the number should read 7714."

"Physical seal reads 7174."

"Which is obviously wrong."

"Maybe the manifest is wrong."

"It is the same lot sequence."

"Probably."

He disliked his own word returned to him.

Landing officer hid a smile badly.

I continued.

"Write both. If someone later confirms transcription source, attach that. Don't make current physical number disappear because explanation seems obvious."

He stared.

Then nodded.

"Fine."

That was the work.

Not intelligence theater.

Keep options open for later people with better information.

The landing officer signed my completion slip.

**TRANSFER SEAL WITNESS COMPLETED. RECORD ACCEPTED. NO SEAL BREAK DURING TRANSFER. ONE CRATE MOVED UNDER DISCREPANCY HOLD.**

Five copper.

Ten to fifteen.

Still below thirty.

But purse was not the important part.

I now had:

one district field-chain verification;

one completed transfer-witness verification;

Morrow contract-completion request pending;

Bracken final hazard confirmation pending.

That changed the board.

I went back before closing.

The contracting clerk read the new slip.

"Now?"

"Now you qualify for some two-reference custody listings."

He pulled tomorrow's cards.

Not all.

Not route audit.

But warehouse claim inventory, eight copper, two references.

Still required experience with inventory categories I had not done.

He tapped another:

**BONDED LOT DISCREPANCY REVIEW / 7C / ONE DAY / TWO FIELD REFERENCES / NO VALUATION**

That one was closer.

"What does review mean?"

"Compare physical marks, seal history, transfer records, lot sheets. Flag inconsistencies. No opening. No claim decision."

Exactly adjacent to today.

"Available?"

"Tomorrow."

"Can I take it?"

He looked at my two slips.

Then at me.

"You can apply. Hiring office may ask about today's discrepancy hold."

Not guaranteed.

Good.

I applied.

If accepted, seven copper.

If completed, stronger custody record.

That was a ladder.

Not glamorous.

But ladders were useful even when you had one leg.

I did not write that down.

Some jokes deserved mercy.

Late afternoon, I had fifteen copper again.

Five above floor.

Same place I'd been before returning Lakeward archive.

If I judged only purse, today looked like repair work on my own bad financial decisions.

Wrong.

Yesterday I could not credibly apply for the seven-copper review.

Today I could.

That was progression.

Earning ceiling moved before wealth did.

I sat outside the contracting office and looked at the other lanes in my head.

Magic still mattered.

Body still mattered.

The work leg might eventually be the biggest multiplier of all.

People could dwarf everything if the right connection appeared.

Artifacts could rewrite rules.

Adventure could produce all of those at once.

But right now, money was the lever I could actually pull repeatedly.

So I would pull it until another lever became better.

That was strategy.

Not devotion.

I went past the museum road on way back to bunkhouse.

Did not go in.

Passed artifact stalls.

Did not browse.

That surprised me more than it should have.

Not because artifacts stopped mattering.

Because looking at things I couldn't buy was no longer the best use of afternoon.

At post counter, still no Hessa.

Still no Sori.

Still no Hark & Venn.

Still no home reply.

Distance remained distance.

I could not optimize mail by staring at pigeon architecture.

So I didn't.

Dinner.

Bits.

Bunkhouse.

No trial-limb wear today.

Shoulder had worked enough during landing transfer.

Magic unchanged.

Fifty-nine.

Fifty-three.

One hundred thirty-nine.

I opened notebook.

Not six headings.

One.

**LEVER: EARNING POWER**

Under it:

**Why:** buys time, mobility, tools, travel, specialist access, opportunity capture. Current bottleneck is not ability to create value. It is proof that lets market price me above labor.

Then:

**Today:**

District chain verification secured.

Morrow completion request sent.

Bracken hazard timing notation secured, final pending.

Transfer seal witness completed, +5c.

Second field verification secured.

7c bonded discrepancy review application submitted.

Cash 15c.

Thirty target unchanged.

Then one last line.

**Reassess when money stops unlocking the most doors.**

That mattered.

I had been poor long enough to confuse money with freedom.

It wasn't.

It was one component of freedom.

A powerful one.

Old me had known that too.

The best support plans never worshipped one resource.

They identified what the situation was short on and fixed that first.

Right now, I was short on capital and on the reputation required to earn it efficiently.

Fine.

Problem named.

Work started.

Tomorrow, if the seven-copper review accepted me, I would take it.

If it didn't, I would find the next move that built the same ladder.

And if something genuinely higher leverage appeared before then?

I would change plans.

That was the part people misunderstood about strategy.

A plan wasn't a promise to keep being wrong.

# CHAPTER 472

## THE BROKER

Seven copper was not enough.

That was the thought I woke up with.

Not because seven copper was bad money.

Yesterday five copper for half a day had felt like proof that I could climb out of the one-copper, two-copper sludge I'd been treating as normal.

Seven was better.

Eight was better.

Ten would be better still.

And all of them were the wrong scale.

I lay on the bunkhouse floor staring at the underside of the cot above me and remembered silver.

Actual silver.

Not as a mythical currency rich people whispered about while I scrubbed ferry tags.

I'd had silver before.

I'd wasted silver before.

I'd won silver before.

At one point I'd handled an object I had confidently described as worth forty gold.

Forty.

Gold.

Maybe I'd been right. Maybe I'd been catastrophically full of shit. The important part was that my brain had once been willing to think at that scale.

Now I was excited because a board might let me earn seven copper if two clerks liked my paperwork.

Healthy was not the same thing as ambitious.

That landed harder than I wanted.

The last months had been healthy.

Learn the city.

Pay what I owed.

Don't gamble food money.

Take work I could actually do.

Build relationships.

Don't pretend I was still S-class.

Don't buy every shiny thing.

Recover from mistakes.

Travel.

See the world.

All good.

All necessary.

Also autopilot.

A very responsible autopilot.

Still autopilot.

I sat up.

My shoulder objected mildly.

"Yeah. You too."

The man on the cot above me shifted.

I decided not to explain.

At the district board, the Bonded Lot Discrepancy Review had my application tag moved from **PENDING** to **APPROVED WITH SCOPE LIMIT**.

Seven copper.

One day.

No valuation.

No opening sealed goods.

No ownership determination.

I stared at it.

Then took my application tag down.

The clerk looked over.

"You're declining?"

"Yes."

"You applied yesterday."

"I was younger."

He waited.

"By one day."

"Transformative period."

He did not laugh.

Good.

"Reason?"

I looked at the board.

Seven copper was a perfectly rational next rung.

Which was exactly the problem.

"I'm trying to figure out whether I'm climbing the wrong ladder."

That got his attention.

"Seven copper is more than five."

"I did notice."

"Then what ladder do you want?"

"The one where I stop selling days."

He leaned back.

There.

That was the sentence.

I hadn't known it until I said it.

The clerk glanced toward the private notices at far end of room.

"Then you're looking at trade, commission, brokerage, appraisal, ownership, or management."

"Which one requires the least money to start?"

"Brokerage if somebody trusts you. Commission if somebody needs you. Management if somebody is foolish."

"Appraisal?"

"Training, registration, liability."

"Trade?"

"Capital."

"Ownership?"

"More capital."

Good.

There was the actual map.

"Brokerage."

He shrugged.

"People hear that word and imagine standing between two idiots while money falls out. Usually you're standing between two people who each know their own business better than you and both think your fee is too high."

"Sounds social."

"It's mostly finding mismatch. Somebody has the wrong thing. Somebody else needs it. Or somebody knows something in one market that another market hasn't priced yet. Then you make the introduction, carry the risk, or buy the spread."

Arbitrage.

There it was.

Not the word.
